Drell-Yan angular lepton distributions at small 𝒙 x from TMD factorization. Preprint: JLAB-THY-21-3407
Ian Balitsky

Abstract
The Drell-Yan process is studied in the framework of TMD factorization in the Sudakov region s ≫ Q 2 ≫ q ⟂ 2 s\gg Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2} corresponding to recent LHC experiments with Q 2 Q^{2} of order of mass of Z-boson and transverse momentum of DY pair ∼ \sim few tens GeV. The DY hadronic tensors are expressed in terms of quark and quark-gluon TMDs with 1 Q 2 {1\over Q^{2}} and 1 N c 2 {1\over N_{c}^{2}} accuracy. It is demonstrated that in the leading order in N c N_{c} the higher-twist quark-quark-gluon TMDs reduce to leading-twist TMDs due to QCD equation of motion. The resulting hadronic tensors depend on two leading-twist TMDs: f 1 f_{1} responsible for total DY cross section, and Boer-Mulders function h 1 ⟂ h_{1}^{\perp} . The corresponding qualitative and semi-quantitative predictions seem to agree with LHC data on five angular coefficients A 0 − A 4 A_{0}-A_{4} of DY pair production. The remaining three coefficients A 5 − A 7 A_{5}-A_{7} are determined by quark-quark-gluon TMDs multiplied by extra 1 N c {1\over N_{c}} so they appear to be relatively small in accordance with LHC results.



1
Introduction

The Drell-Yan (DY) process
[
1
]
has been extensively studied in high energy physics field for precise tests of QCD, investigation of the structure of the proton,
and searches for possible new physics. Since the advent of high-energy colliders, the attention shifted to processes with
large invariant mass of DY pair produced both by photon and
Z
-boson.
The important part of these studies is the transverse-momentum dependence of angular distribution of DY lepton pairs with l
arge invariant mass produced in hadronic collisions. It was extensively studied in the framework of collinear
factorization
[
2
,
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
]

leading to good agreement with experiment
[
8
]
.
If one considers, however, the DY process at transverse momentum of lepton pair much smaller than their invariant mass, the collinear factorization
should be replaced by TMD factorization
[
9
,
10
,
11
,
12
,
13
]
.
In this paper the DY process will be studied in so-called Sudakov plus small-
x
region
s\gg Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
where

Q^{2}
is the invariant mass of DY pair and
q_{\perp}
is the transverse momentum of produced leptons. The typical case is the lepton pair
production at LHC with DY invariant mass in the vicinity of
Z
-boson and transverse momentum of DY pair of order of ten or few tens of GeV.

This paper is the third in a series of papers devoted to description of DY process in terms of TMD rapidity factorization.
In the first paper
[
14
]
, A. Tarasov and the author calculated power corrections
to the total cross section of
Z
-boson production using the method developed in earlier paper
[
15
]
.
The second paper
[
16
]
was devoted to calculation of angular coefficients
for DY process mediated by virtual photon. Unfortunately, it is hard to compare these results with experiment due to the fact
that the LHC measurements of angular distributions
are performed at the invariant mass
\sim
100 GeV where the contribution of
Z
-boson is dominant.
The present paper is devoted to generalization of the approach of papers
[
14
]
and
[
16
]
to
angular distributions of DY pair production by unpolarized protons at LHC kinematics.

The differential cross section of DY process is determined by the sum of products of leptonic tensors and hadronic tensors.
The leptonic tensors are given by simple first-order EW diagrams while hadronic tensors are determined by QCD correlation functions

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{1\over(2\pi)^{4}}\!\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|j_{\mu}(x)j_{\nu}(0)|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle.

(1)

where
p_{A},p_{B}
are hadron momenta,
q
is the momentum of DY pair, and
j_{\mu}
is either electromagnetic or
Z
-boson current.

As was mentioned above, a golden standard of QCD analysis of such hadronic tensors in the region where transverse momenta are much smaller than the invariant mass of the DY pair
is the TMD factorization. The leading-twist hadronic tensors can be represented as

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054ptW_{i}~=~\sum_{\rm flavors}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{\cal D}_{f/A}^{(i)}(x_{A},k_{\perp}){\cal D}_{f/B}^{(i)}(x_{B},q_{\perp}-k_{\perp})C_{i}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt+~{\rm power~corrections}~+~{\rm Y-terms}

(2)

where
{\cal D}_{f/A}(x_{A},k_{\perp})
is the TMD density of a parton
f
in hadron
A

with fraction of momentum
x_{A}
and transverse momentum
k_{\perp}
,
{\cal D}_{f/B}(x_{B},q_{\perp}-k_{\perp})
is a similar quantity for hadron
B
, and
coefficient functions
C_{i}(q,k)
are determined by the cross section
\sigma(ff\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})

of production of DY pair of invariant mass
q^{2}
in the scattering of two partons.
The DY angular distributions are conventionally parametrized by eight angular coefficients
A_{i}
in Collins-Soper frame
[
2
]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{d\sigma\over dQ^{2}dyd\Omega_{l}}~=~{3\over 16\pi}{d\sigma\over dQ^{2}dy}\Big[(1+c_{\theta}^{2})+{A_{0}\over 2}(1-3c_{\theta}^{2})+A_{1}s_{2\theta}c_{\phi}+{A_{2}\over 2}s_{\theta}^{2}c_{2\phi}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~A_{3}s_{\theta}c_{\phi}+A_{4}c_{\theta}+A_{5}s_{\theta}^{2}s_{2\phi}+A_{6}s_{2\theta}s_{\phi}+A_{7}s_{\theta}s_{\phi}\Big]

(3)

where
y
is the rapidity of DY pair and
c_{\phi}\equiv\cos\phi
,
s_{\phi}\equiv\sin\phi
etc.
The aim of this paper is to express
A_{i}(Q^{2},q_{\perp}^{2})
at Sudakov kinematics
s\gg Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
in terms of TMDs and compare
(at least qualitatively) to ATLAS
[
17
]
and CMS
[
18
]
measurements.

Unfortunately, the TMD analysis of Drell-Yan angular distributions
A_{i}

is hindered by the fact that not all hadronic tensors are determined by leading-twist quark-antiquark TMDs which have parton interpretation.
Some tensor structures in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
1
) are determined by power corrections to leading-twist TMDs expressed in terms of quark-antiquark-gluon distributions
which are virtually unknown.
Fortunately, as demonstrated recently in Ref.
[
14
]
, at the leading order in
N_{c}

these power corrections are still determined by
leading-twist TMDs.
Moreover, at small
x_{A}
and
x_{B}
the majority of hadronic tensors
depends only on two leading-twist TMDs:
f_{1}
responsible for total DY cross section,
and Boer-Mulders function
h_{1}^{\perp}
. The rest of of hadronic tensors determined by power corrections
due to quark-antiquark-gluon distributions are down by at least one
{1\over N_{c}}
factor which seems to qualitatively agree with LHC measurements
of angular distributions.

Note that in addition to power corrections due to QCD dynamics, there are fiducial power corrections arising from fiducial cuts on experimental measurements.
These cuts introduce linear power corrections in
q_{\perp}/Q
, see the discussion in Ref.
[
19
]
. In this paper we do not consider fiducial power corrections.

The paper is organized as follows. In Sect.
2
I set up the notations, present the formula for differential cross section of DY lepton pair, and
list the relevant hadronic tensors. In Sect.
3
I briefly outline the method of calculation of power corrections to hadronic tensors
developed in Refs.
[
14
]
and
[
15
]
. Sect.
4
contains the streamlined calculation of photon-mediated DY
process which is used as a reference point to calculation of
Z
-mediated and interference terms in Sections
5
and
6
. The results
for hadronic tensors and angular coefficients are presented in Sect.
7
whic also contains the comparison to LHC measurements.
Conclusions are summarized in Sect.
8
and the necessary technical details are listed in the Appendix.

2
Drell-Yan cross section in the Sudakov region

At high energies, the production of a neutral
e^{+}e^{-}
(or
\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
) pair in hadron-hadron collisions is mediated by virtual photon or by
Z
-boson, see Fig. 1

Figure 1:
Typical annihilation-type (a) and exchange-type (b) diagrams for DY particle production.

\displaystyle h_{A}(p_{A})+h_{B}(p_{B})\to\gamma,Z(q)+X\to l_{1}(l)+l_{2}(l^{\prime})+X,

(4)

where
h_{A,B}
denote the colliding hadrons with momenta
p_{A}
and
P_{B}
and
l_{1,2}
denote the outgoing lepton pair with total momentum
q=l+l^{\prime}
.
To avoid cluttering of
\mu
’s if our formulas, we will consider production of
e^{+}e^{-}
pairs, the results for
\mu^{+}\mu^{-}
pairs are the same.

The relevant terms of the Lagrangian for quark fields
\psi^{f}
are

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{\gamma}~=~e\!\int\!d^{4}x~J_{\mu}A^{\mu}(x),~~~~~~~~~J_{\mu}~=~{\bar{e}}\gamma_{\mu}e-\sum_{\rm flavors}e_{f}{\bar{\psi}}^{f}\gamma_{\mu}\psi^{f}

(5)

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{Z}~=~e\!\int\!d^{4}x~{\cal J}_{\mu}Z^{\mu}(x),~~~~~~~~~{\cal J}_{\mu}~=~c_{e}{\bar{e}}(a_{e}-\gamma_{5})e-\sum_{\rm flavors}c_{f}{\bar{\psi}}^{f}\gamma_{\mu}(a_{f}-\gamma_{5})\psi^{f}

where
e
is the positron charge and

\displaystyle c_{u,c}~=~{1\over 4c_{W}s_{W}},~~~a_{u,c}~=~1-{8\over 3}s_{W}^{2},~~~c_{d,s}~=~-{1\over 4c_{W}s_{W}},~~~a_{d,s}~=~1-{4\over 3}s_{W}^{2},

\displaystyle c_{e}~=~{1\over 4c_{W}s_{W}},~~~a_{e}~=~1-4s_{W}^{2},~~~~~~~c_{W}\equiv\cos\theta_{W},~~~s_{W}\equiv\sin\theta_{W}.

(6)

The differential cross section of production of pair of leptons with momenta
l
and
l^{\prime}

by scattering of two unpolarized protons is given by

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptd\sigma~=~{d^{4}q\over 2s}\!\int\!{d^{3}ld^{3}l^{\prime}\delta(q-l-l^{\prime})\over(2\pi)^{6}E_{l}E^{\prime}_{l}}e^{4}\!\int\!dx~e^{-iqx}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[A^{\lambda}J_{\lambda}(x)+Z^{\lambda}{\cal J}_{\lambda}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\!\int\!dx^{\prime}dy^{\prime}\big[A^{\mu}{\bar{e}}\gamma_{\mu}e(x^{\prime})+c_{e}Z^{\mu}{\bar{e}}\gamma_{\mu}(a_{e}-\gamma_{5})e(x^{\prime})\big]\sum_{X}|X\rangle\langle X|

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\sum_{s,s^{\prime}}|l,s;l^{\prime},s^{\prime}\rangle\langle l,s;l^{\prime},s^{\prime}|\big[A^{\nu}{\bar{e}}\gamma_{\nu}e(x^{\prime})+c_{e}Z^{\nu}{\bar{e}}\gamma_{\nu}(a_{e}-\gamma_{5})e(x^{\prime})\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~[A^{\rho}J_{\rho}(0)+Z^{\rho}{\cal J}_{\rho}(0)]|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle

(7)

where
\sum_{X}
denotes summation over all intermediate hadron states.
Performing contractions to get photon, Z-boson and lepton propagators, one obtains

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptd\sigma~=~{d^{4}q\over 2s}\!\int\!{d^{3}ld^{3}l^{\prime}\delta(q-l-l^{\prime})\over\pi^{2}E_{l}E^{\prime}_{l}}{e^{4}\over N_{c}}\Big[{1\over q^{4}}L^{\mu\nu}W_{\mu\nu}(q)

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt+~{c_{e}^{2}\over|m_{Z}^{2}-q^{2}|^{2}+\Gamma_{Z}^{2}m_{Z}^{2}}\big[(a_{e}^{2}+1)L^{\mu\nu}-2ia_{e}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}l_{\lambda}{l^{\prime}}_{\rho}\big]W^{\rm Z}_{\mu\nu}(q)

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt+~2c_{e}{(q^{2}-m_{Z}^{2})/q^{2}\over|m_{Z}^{2}-q^{2}|^{2}+\Gamma_{Z}^{2}m_{Z}^{2}}W^{\rm I1}_{\mu\nu}(q)\big[a_{e}L^{\mu\nu}-i\epsilon^{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}l_{\lambda}{l^{\prime}}_{\rho}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt+~2c_{e}{i\Gamma_{Z}m_{Z}/q^{2}\over|m_{Z}^{2}-q^{2}|^{2}+\Gamma_{Z}^{2}m_{Z}^{2}}W^{\rm I2}_{\mu\nu}(q)\big[a_{e}L^{\mu\nu}-i\epsilon^{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}l_{\lambda}{l^{\prime}}_{\rho}\big]

(8)

where
L_{\mu\nu}=l_{\mu}l^{\prime}_{\nu}+l^{\prime}_{\mu}l_{\nu}-g_{\mu\nu}l\cdot l^{\prime}
and hadronic tensors
W^{i}_{\mu\nu}
are
defined as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{i}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{1\over(2\pi)^{4}}\!\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}W^{i}_{\mu\nu}(x)~~~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~N_{c}\langle A,B|J_{\mu}(x)J_{\nu}(0)|A,B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm Z}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~N_{c}\langle A,B|{\cal J}_{\mu}(x){\cal J}_{\nu}(0)|A,B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I1}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2}\langle A,B|J_{\mu}(x){\cal J}_{\nu}(0)+{\cal J}_{\mu}(x)J_{\nu}(0)|A,B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I2}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2}\langle A,B|{\cal J}_{\mu}(x)J_{\nu}(0)-J_{\mu}(x){\cal J}_{\nu}(0)|A,B\rangle

(9)

Hereafter
|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle\equiv|A,B\rangle
for brevity.
Note that hadronic tensors are defined with an extra
N_{c}
so
that the leading-twist contribution will be
\sim N_{c}^{0}
.

To convolute with leptonic tensors, we need to find symmetric and antisymmetric parts separately so we define

\begin{array}[]{lll}W^{\rm ZS}_{\mu\nu}~=~{1\over 2}(W^{\rm Z}_{\mu\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu),&&W^{\rm ZA}_{\mu\nu}~=~{1\over 2}(W^{\rm Z}_{\mu\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu),\\
W^{\rm I1S}_{\mu\nu}~=~{1\over 2}(W^{\rm I1}_{\mu\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu),&&W^{\rm I1A}_{\mu\nu}~=~{1\over 2}(W^{\rm I1}_{\mu\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu),\\
W^{\rm I2S}_{\mu\nu}~=~{1\over 2}(W^{\rm I2}_{\mu\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu),&&W^{\rm I2A}_{\mu\nu}~=~{1\over 2}(W^{\rm I2}_{\mu\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu).\end{array}

(10)

We will calculate these hadronic tensors with
O\big({1\over Q^{2}}\big)
accuracy and express them in terms of TMDs like Eq. (
2
).
It turns out that each
W^{i}
is a sum of three parts

W^{i}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~(W_{1})^{i}_{\mu\nu}(q)+(W_{2})^{i}_{\mu\nu}(q)+(W^{\rm ex})^{i}_{\mu\nu}(q)

(11)

The first part is determined by leading-twist TMDs
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
as mentioned in the Introduction and satisfies the condition

1
1

1

Strictly speaking, the Z-boson current is not conserved so one should not expect
q^{\mu}(W_{1})^{\rm Z,I}_{\mu\nu}(q)=0
.
However, if we consider quarks to be massless, the non-conservation is due to axial anomaly so the corresponding terms in
q^{\mu}(W_{1})^{i}_{\mu\nu}(q)

will be proportional to
\langle p_{A},p_{B}|\alpha_{s}F_{\mu\nu}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}(x)j_{\nu}(0)|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle
. Such matrix elements will be non-zero only at the two-loop level
O(\alpha_{s}^{2})
which is beyond the accuracy of this paper.

q^{\mu}(W_{1})^{i}_{\mu\nu}(q)=0

(12)

The two remaining terms
(W_{2})^{i}_{\mu\nu}
and
(W^{\rm ex})^{i}_{\mu\nu}
are power corrections

\sim O\big({q_{\perp}^{2}g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}\over Q^{2}},{q^{\perp}_{\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}\over Q^{2}}\big)
which come from the diagrams of the Fig.
1
a,b type, respectively.
They
are expressed in terms of quark-antiquark-gluon matrix elements
which cannot be reduced to leading-twist TMDs. The term
(W_{2})^{i}_{\mu\nu}
is
\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
and
\sim N_{c}^{0}
as
while
(W^{\rm ex})^{i}_{\mu\nu}
is
\sim{1\over N_{c}}
. On the other hand, since

(W^{\rm ex})^{i}_{\mu\nu}
comes from exchange-type diagrams it may be numerically larger than
(W_{2})^{i}_{\mu\nu}(q)
coming from annihilation-type diagrams.

3
TMD factorization from rapidity factorization

We use
Sudakov variables
p=\alpha p_{1}+\beta p_{2}+p_{\perp}
, where
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are light-like vectors close to
p_{A}
and
p_{B}
so that

p_{A}=p_{1}+{m^{2}\over s}p_{2}
and
p_{A}=p_{1}+{m^{2}\over s}p_{2}
with
m
being the proton mass.
Also, we use the notations
x_{\bullet}\equiv x_{\mu}p_{1}^{\mu}
and
x_{\star}\equiv x_{\mu}p_{2}^{\mu}

for the dimensionless light-cone coordinates (
x_{\star}=\sqrt{s\over 2}x_{+}
and
x_{\bullet}=\sqrt{s\over 2}x_{-}
). Our metric is
g^{\mu\nu}~=~(1,-1,-1,-1)

which we will frequently rewrite as a sum of longitudinal part and transverse part:

g^{\mu\nu}~=~g_{\parallel}^{\mu\nu}+g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp}~=~{2\over s}\big(p_{1}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}+p_{2}^{\mu}p_{1}^{\nu})+g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}

(13)

Consequently,
p\cdot q~=~(\alpha_{p}\beta_{q}+\alpha_{q}\beta_{p}){s\over 2}-(p,q)_{\perp}
where
(p,q)_{\perp}\equiv-p_{i}q^{i}
.
Throughout the paper, the sum over the Latin indices
i
,
j
, … runs over two transverse components while the sum over Greek indices
\mu
,
\nu
, … runs over four components as usual.

Following Ref.
[
15
]
we separate quark and gluon fields into three sectors (see figure
2
):
“projectile” fields
A_{\mu},\psi_{A}

with
|\beta|<\sigma_{p}
,
“target” fields
B_{\mu},\psi_{B}
with
|\alpha|<\sigma_{t}
and “central rapidity” fields
C_{\mu},\psi_{C}
with
|\alpha|>\sigma_{t}
and
|\beta|>\sigma_{p}
,
see Fig.
2
.

2
2

2

Although the kinematics is best suited for LHC collider, I call
A
hadron “projectile” and
B
hadron
“target” for convenience.

Figure 2:
Rapidity factorization for DY particle production

Our goal is to integrate over central fields and get
the amplitude in the factorized form, i.e. as a product of functional integrals over
A
fields representing projectile matrix elements (TMDs of the projectile)
and functional integrals over
B
fields representing target matrix elements (TMDs of the target).
In the spirit of background-field method, we “freeze” projectile and target fields and get a sum of diagrams in these external fields.
Since
|\beta|<\sigma_{p}
in the projectile fields and
|\alpha|<\sigma_{t}
in the target fields, at the tree level
one can set with power accuracy
\beta=0
for the projectile fields and
\alpha=0
for the target fields - the corrections will
be
O\big({m^{2}\over\sigma_{p}s}\big)
and
O\big({m^{2}\over\sigma_{t}s}\big)
.
In the coordinate space, projectile fields depend on
x_{\bullet}
and
x_{\perp}
and target ones on
x_{\star}
and
x_{\perp}
.
Beyond the tree level, the integration over
C
fields produces
logarithms of the cutoffs
\sigma_{p}
and
\sigma_{t}
which match the corresponding
logs in TMDs of the projectile and the target, see the discussion in Ref.
[
16
]

As discussed in Ref.
[
16
]
, central fields at the tree level are given by a set of Feynman diagrams with
retarded
propagators
in background field
A+B
and
\psi_{A}+\psi_{B}
, see figure
3
.

Figure 3:
Typical diagram for the classical field with projectile/target sources. The Green functions of central fields are given by retarded propagators.

3
3

3

We take into account only
u,d,s,c
quarks and consider them massless. In principle, one can include “massless”
b
-quark for
q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m_{b}^{2}
.

The set of such “retarded” diagrams represent the solution of QCD equations of motion with sources being projectile and target fields.
After summation of these diagrams the hadronic tensor (
1
) can be represented as

\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}~=~\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{4}}\!\int\!d^{4}xe^{-iqx}\sum_{m,n}\!\int\!dz_{m}c_{m,n}(q,x)\langle p_{A}|\hat{\Phi}_{A}(z_{m})|p_{A}\rangle\!\int\!dz^{\prime}_{n}\langle p_{B}|\hat{\Phi}_{B}(z^{\prime}_{n})|p_{B}\rangle.

(14)

where
c_{m,n}
are coefficients and
\Phi
can be any of the background fields promoted to operators after integration over
projectile and target fields.

In general, the summation of diagrams of Fig.
3
type is a formidable task which still awaits its solution. Fortunately, as demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
,
at our kinematics we have a small parameter
{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}\ll 1
and it is possible to expand classical solution for central fields in powers of this parameter.

Now we expand the classical quark and gluon fields in powers of
{p_{\perp}^{2}\over p_{\parallel}^{2}}\sim{m_{\perp}^{2}\over s}
.
It is convenient to choose a gauge where
A_{\star}=0
for projectile fields and
B_{\bullet}=0
for target fields.
(The existence of such gauge was proved in appendix B of Ref.
[
15
]
by explicit construction.)
As demonstrated in Ref.
[
14
]
,
expanding it in powers of
p^{2}_{\perp}/p_{\parallel}^{2}
we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\Psi(x)~=~\Psi_{1}(x)+\Psi_{2}(x)+\dots,

(15)

where

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\Psi_{1}~=~\psi_{A}+\Xi_{1},~~~~\Xi_{1}~=~-{\not{p}_{2}\over s}\gamma^{i}B_{i}{1\over\alpha+i\epsilon}\psi_{A}~=~{i\over s}\sigma_{\star i}B^{i}{1\over\alpha+i\epsilon}\psi_{A},

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}~=~\bar{\psi}_{A}+{\bar{\Xi}}_{1},~~~~{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}~=~-\big(\bar{\psi}_{A}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\big)\gamma^{i}B_{i}{\not{p}_{2}\over s}~=~-{i\over s}\big(\bar{\psi}_{A}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\big)B^{i}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\Psi_{2}~=~\psi_{B}+\Xi_{2},~~~~\Xi_{2}~=~-{\not{p}_{1}\over s}\gamma^{i}A_{i}{1\over\beta+i\epsilon}\psi_{B}~=~{i\over s}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}A^{i}{1\over\beta+i\epsilon}\psi_{B},

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}~=~\bar{\psi}_{B}+{\bar{\Xi}}_{2},~~~~{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}~=~-\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta-i\epsilon}\big)\gamma^{i}A_{i}{\not{p}_{1}\over s}~=~-{i\over s}\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta-i\epsilon}\big)A_{i}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}

(16)

and dots stand for terms subleading in
{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
and/or
\alpha_{q},\beta_{q}
parameters. In this formula

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over\alpha+i\epsilon}\psi_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\equiv~-i\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}~\psi_{A}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\Big({\bar{\psi}}_{A}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\Big)(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\equiv~i\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}~{\bar{\psi}}_{A}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp})

(17)

and similarly for
{1\over\beta\pm i\epsilon}
.
For brevity, in what follows we denote
\big(\bar{\psi}_{A}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\equiv\big({\bar{\psi}}_{A}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\big)(x)
and

\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta}\big)(x)\equiv\big({\bar{\psi}}_{B}{1\over\beta-i\epsilon}\big)(x)
.
The corresponding expansion of classical gluon fields is presented in Ref.
[
15
]
, but we do not need it here.

4
4

4

Since we are dealing with tree approximation and quark equations of motion, it is convenient to include coupling constant
g

in the definition of gluon fields.

Let us estimate the relative size of corrections
\Xi
in Eq. (
16
) at small
x
. As we will see,
{1\over\alpha}
and
{1\over\beta}
transform
to
{1\over\alpha_{q}}
and
{1\over\beta_{q}}
in our TMDs so

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\Xi_{1}~\sim~\psi_{A}{m_{\perp}\over\alpha_{q}\sqrt{s}}~\sim~\psi_{A}{q_{\perp}\over Q},~~~~\Xi_{2}~\sim~\psi_{B}{m_{\perp}\over\beta_{q}\sqrt{s}}~\sim~\psi_{B}{q_{\perp}\over Q}

(18)

if
\alpha_{q}\sim\beta_{q}\sim{Q\over\sqrt{s}}
(recall that we assume that the DY pair is emitted in the central region of rapidity).
For example, the correction
\sim[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}]
will be of order of
{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
in comparison to leading-twist contribution
[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}]
.

5
5

5

The reader may wonder why there are no corrections
\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
coming from next terms in the expansion (
15
)
like
[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}{\gamma^{i}\over s}\not{p}_{2}{1\over\beta}{1\over\alpha}\gamma^{j}\partial_{i}B_{j}\Psi_{1}(0)]
. The reason is that

{1\over\beta}
between
{\bar{\psi}}_{B}(0)
and
B_{j}(0)
does
not
transform to
{1\over\beta_{q}}
and remains
\sim O(1)
, see the discussion
in the Appendix 8.3.4 of Ref.
[
14
]
.

As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
, the relevant terms contributions to hadronic tensors (
9
) with this accuracy are

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptg_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}\Big\{1,~{q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}\Big\},~~{q_{\perp}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big\{1,~{q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}\Big\},~~g_{\parallel}^{\mu\nu}\Big\{1,~{q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}\Big\},~~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over\beta_{q}s}\big(p_{1}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}\pm~p_{1}^{\nu}q_{\perp}^{\mu}\big),~~{1\over\alpha_{q}s}\big(p_{2}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}\pm~p_{2}^{\nu}q_{\perp}^{\mu}\big),~~q_{\perp}^{2}{p_{1}^{\mu}p_{1}^{\nu}\over\beta_{q}^{2}s^{2}},~~q_{\perp}^{2}{p_{2}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}\over\alpha_{q}^{2}s^{2}}

(19)

Let us also specify the terms which we do not calculate. Roughly speaking, they correspond to terms in Eq. (
19
) multiplied by

{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
or by either
\alpha_{q}
or
\beta_{q}
.

In addition, in this paper we will consider only leading-
N_{c}
power corrections. As we will see below, leading-twist hadronic tensor is
\sim N_{c}^{0}

and power corrections can be
\sim N_{c}^{0}
,
\sim{1\over N_{c}}
, or
{1\over N_{c}^{2}}
. The corrections corrections
\sim{1\over N_{c}^{2}}
were found in Ref.
[
14
]

for the case of total cross section, i.e. for Eq. (
8
) integrated over
l
.
In this paper such corrections
\sim{1\over N_{c}^{2}}
will be neglected.

Thus, the calculation of power corrections with our accuracy boils down to calculation of tensors (
9
) with
\psi\rightarrow\Psi_{1}+\Psi_{2}
.
In the next sections we will
consider five lines in Eq. (
8
) for the differential cross section.

4
Hadronic tensor for photon-mediated DY process

In this Section I briefly summarize the calculation of
W^{f}_{\mu\nu}
performed in Ref.
[
16
]
paying attention only to terms
giving non-negligible contributions listed in Eq. (
19
) at the leading-
N_{c}
level.
The reason is that hadronic tensors listed in Eq. (
9
) differ from
W_{\mu\nu}
by
replacement(s)
\gamma_{\mu(\nu)}\rightarrow\gamma_{\mu(\nu)}\gamma_{5}
and/or
\mu,\nu
antisymmetrization instead of symmetrization. Both operations
do not change power counting in
{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
and
\alpha_{q},\beta_{q}
parameters so the calculation of the rest of the terms in (
9
) will be based
on the calculation of (non-negligible) contributions to in
W_{\mu\nu}
outlined in this Section.

In this Section we take into account hadronic tensor due to electromagnetic currents of
u,d,s,c
quarks and consider these quarks to be massless.
It is convenient to define coordinate-space hadronic tensor multiplied by
{2\over s}
(and denoted by extra “check” mark) as follows

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}(x)~\equiv~{2\over s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|J_{\mu}(x)J_{\nu}(0)|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle

(20)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{s/2\over(2\pi)^{4}}\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}\check{W}_{\mu\nu}(x).

For future use, let us also define the hadronic tensor in mixed representation: in momentum longitudinal
space but in transverse coordinate space

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{i(q,x)_{\perp}}W_{\mu\nu}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},x_{\perp}),

(21)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},x_{\perp})~\equiv~{1\over(2\pi)^{4}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\star}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}-i\beta_{q}x_{\star}}\check{W}_{\mu\nu}(x_{\bullet},x_{\star},x_{\perp}).

With the definition (
20
), power counting of contributions to
\check{W}_{\mu\nu}(x_{\bullet},x_{\star},x_{\perp})

will mirror that of
W_{\mu\nu}(q)
terms without extra
{1\over s}
factor.

After integration over central fields in the tree approximation we obtain

\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}(x)~\equiv~N_{c}{2\over s}\langle A,B|J_{\mu}(x_{\bullet},x_{\star},x_{\perp})J_{\nu}(0)|A,B\rangle

(22)

where

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-J^{\mu}~=~J^{\mu}_{1}+J^{\mu}_{2}+J^{\mu}_{12}+J^{\mu}_{21},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptJ^{\mu}_{1}~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}\bar{\Psi}_{1}^{f}\gamma^{\mu}\Psi_{1}^{f},~~~J^{\mu}_{12}~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}\bar{\Psi}_{1}^{f}\gamma^{\mu}\Psi_{2}^{f}

(23)

and similarly for
J^{\mu}_{2}
and
J^{\mu}_{21}
. Here

\langle A,B|{\cal O}(\psi_{A},A_{\mu},\psi_{B},B_{\mu})|A,B\rangle
denotes
double functional integral over
A
and
B
fields which gives matrix elements between projectile and target states of Eq. (
14
) type.

The leading-twist contribution to
W_{\mu\nu}(q)
comes only from annihilation-type
product
J_{12}^{\mu}(x)J_{21}^{\nu}(0)+1\leftrightarrow 2

while power corrections may come also from
J_{1}^{\mu}(x)J_{2}^{\nu}(0)+1\leftrightarrow 2
.
As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
,
power corrections from

J_{1}^{\mu}(x)J_{2}^{\nu}(0)
terms are down by one power of
N_{c}
in comparison to leading-
N_{c}
terms. On the other hand,
they come from exchange-type diagrams like Fig.
1
b so they are determined by product of two quark distributions (one with additional gluon)
rather than from annihilation-type diagrams in Fig.
1
a proportional to product of quark and antiquark distributions.

We will first calculate annihilation-type contributions coming from

J_{12}^{\mu}(x)J_{21}^{\nu}(0)+1\leftrightarrow 2
terms. Since leptonic tensor
L_{\mu\nu}
is symmetric,
we consider

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\check{W}^{{\rm a}}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|J_{12}^{\mu}(x)J_{21}^{\nu}(0)+J_{21}^{\mu}(x)J_{12}^{\nu}(0)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\check{W}^{f}_{\mu\nu}(x),

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\check{W}^{f}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{f}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi_{2}^{f}(x)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{f}(0)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi_{1}^{f}(0)]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

(24)

After Fierz transformations (
284
) and (
286
) they can be sorted out as

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\check{W}^{f}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{{\rm F}f}(q)+\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{{\rm H}f}(q)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm F}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\big(g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}-\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}\big)\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt+~\gamma_{\alpha}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0,

(25)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm H}(x)~=~\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm G}(x)+\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm T}(x)

(26)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm G}(x)~=~-g_{\mu\nu}{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt-~[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0,

(27)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm T}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\big(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-{1\over 2}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt\times~\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)]{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\beta}^{~\xi}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

(28)

for flavor
f
which we are considering. As discussed in Sect.
3
,
x_{\star}
in projectile matrix elements is set to be zero and similarly
for
x_{\bullet}=0
in target matrix elements. To save space, we will often assume this instead of explicitly displaying.

In the remainder of this Section we will outline calculation of leading power corrections to the above equations starting with
W^{\rm F}
terms.

4.1
W^{\rm F}
contribution

As we discussed in Sect.
3
,
to calculate (
25
) one needs to plug in
\Psi_{i}
in the form (
16
). First, the leading-twist contribution is

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm F,lt}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\big(g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}-\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}\big)\langle p_{A},p_{B}|\big\{[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83385pt+~\gamma_{\alpha}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\big\}|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{1\over 2s}\big(g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}-\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}\big)\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83385pt+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}+x\leftrightarrow 0

(29)

Hereafter we use notations
\langle{\cal O}\rangle_{A}\equiv\langle p_{A}|{\cal O}|p_{A}\rangle
and

\langle{\cal O}\rangle_{B}\equiv\langle p_{B}|{\cal O}|p_{B}\rangle
for brevity. Using parametrizations (
322
) and (
323
)
we can write down the corresponding contribution to
W^{F}_{\mu\nu}
in the form

\hskip 0.0ptW_{\mu\nu}^{\rm F,lt}(q)~=~{1\over 16\pi^{4}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\star}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}-i\beta_{q}x_{\star}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}\check{W}^{\rm lt}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}~F(q,k_{\perp})

(30)

where

F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~=~f_{1}^{f}\big(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}\big){\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}\big(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp}\big)~+~f_{1}^{f}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}

(31)

Here the term with
f\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}
comes from
x\leftrightarrow 0
contribution.

4.1.1
Terms with one quark-quark-gluon TMD (one-gluon terms)

Next, here will be terms with one or two gluon fields in Eq. (
25
) coming from replacement(s) (
16
).
Terms with one gluon are

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm 1F}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\big(g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}-\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}\big)\langle p_{A},p_{B}|\big\{[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]+[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]

(32)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]~+~\gamma_{\alpha}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\big\}|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

Let us consider the first term in the r.h.s. of the above equation. As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
, the only non-negligible contribution
comes from longitudinal
\mu
and transverse
\nu
(or
vice versa
), the term
\sim g_{\mu\nu}
vanishes, and we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{(1){\rm F}}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+~\gamma_{\mu}\otimes\gamma_{\nu}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}|A,B\rangle+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\Big\{\Big[\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt\times~\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\big[\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}{\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{\perp}_{\nu}\psi(x_{\star},x_{\perp})\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+~i\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\sigma_{\star\nu}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}{\not\!\!B}(0){\hat{p}}_{1}\psi(x_{\star},x_{\perp})\rangle_{B}\big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(33)

Here we separated color-singlet contributions and used Eq. (
321
) to reduce number of
\gamma
-matrices.

Using formulas (
329
), (
330
), (
332
), and (
335
) for quark-antiquark-gluon operators and parametrizations
from Sect.
9.2
we get the contribution to
W_{\mu\nu}
in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{(1){\rm F}}_{1\mu\nu}(q)~=~{N_{c}\over 16\pi^{4}}{1\over s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\star}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}-i\beta x_{\star}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}

(34)

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt\times~\langle A,B|\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]+x\leftrightarrow 0|A,B\rangle~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{1\over 64\pi^{6}}{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int\!dx_{\star}d^{2}x^{\prime}_{\perp}e^{-i\beta x_{\star}+i(q-k,x^{\prime})_{\perp}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times~\big[\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}{\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{\perp}_{\nu}\psi(x_{\star},x^{\prime}_{\perp})\rangle_{B}+x\leftrightarrow 0\big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over\alpha_{q}s}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}(q-k)_{\nu}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

where
f_{1}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}_{1}
term in
F
comes from
x\leftrightarrow 0
contribution.

As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
, contribution of the second term in r.h.s. of Eq. (
32
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{(1){\rm F}}_{2\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]+~...

(35)

doubles the result (
34
) and the result for the
third and the fourth terms is obtained from Eq. (
34
) by replacement

{p_{2\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}\over\alpha_{q}s}\leftrightarrow{p_{1\mu}k^{\perp}_{\nu}\over\beta_{q}s}
so

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{(1){\rm F}}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~2\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big({p_{1\mu}k^{\perp}_{\nu}\over\beta_{q}s}+{p_{2\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}\over\alpha_{q}s}\Big)F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

(36)

This result agrees with the corresponding
1/Q
terms in Ref.
[
20
]
.

4.1.2
Terms with two quark-quark-gluon TMDs (two-gluon terms)

Let us now consider terms in
W^{F}_{\mu\nu}
from Eq. (
25
) with two gluon operators. The first of such terms is

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2a)F}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\big(g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}-\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}\big)\langle p_{A},p_{B}|\big\{[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]

(37)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+~[\Xi_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][\Xi_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]+\gamma_{\alpha}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\big\}|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

It is convenient to start from the contribution

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{V}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm 1F}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|\big\{[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt+~\gamma_{\mu}\otimes\gamma_{\nu}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\big\}|A,B\rangle+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=~-{1\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{i}(x)\gamma_{\mu}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{j}(0)\gamma_{\nu}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt+~\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\}~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(38)

As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
, the non-negligible contribution comes only from transverse
\mu
and
\nu
. In this case
we can use formula (
310
) and get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{V}^{\rm 1F}_{\mu_{\perp}\nu_{\perp}}~=~-{1\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{i}(x)\gamma_{\mu_{\perp}}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{j}(0)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+~\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\}~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=~-{g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}\over s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}{\not\!\!A}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}{\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(39)

This gives the contribution of the first matrix element in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
37
) to
W_{\mu\nu}(q)
in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 2}\big(g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}-\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}\big){1\over 16\pi^{4}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\star}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}-i\beta_{q}x_{\star}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}\check{V}_{2\mu_{\perp}\nu_{\perp}}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{g_{\mu\nu}^{\parallel}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(40)

where we again used formulas from Appendices
9.2
and
9.3
. Next, as shown in Ref.
[
16
]
,
the contribution of the second matrix element in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
37
) is equal to that of the first one so we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}^{\rm(2a)F}(q)~=~{2g_{\mu\nu}^{\parallel}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(41)

The second two-gluon contribution to
\check{W}^{F}
in Eq. (
25
) is

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm(2b)F}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{N_{c}\over 2s}(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta})\langle A,B|\big\{[\bar{\psi}_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]+\gamma_{\alpha}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\big\}|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

(42)

Let us consider the first matrix element in the r.h.s. of the above equation. We get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm(2b)F}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{N_{c}\over 2s}(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times~\langle A,B|\big\{[\bar{\psi}_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{1\over s^{3}}(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}p_{1\nu}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}p_{1\mu}-g_{\mu\nu}p_{1}^{\alpha})\Big(\langle\bar{\psi}(x)A_{j}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}A_{i}(0)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times~\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\beta}\big)(0)\gamma^{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\Big)~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{4p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over s^{4}}\Big(\langle\bar{\psi}(x)A_{j}(x){\not\!p}_{2}A_{i}(0)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\beta}\big)(0)\gamma^{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+~\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\Big)

(43)

up to the terms which are negligible as shown in Ref.
[
16
]
.
Using Eq. (
298
) this can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm(2b)F}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{4p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over s^{4}}\Big(\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!\!A}(x){\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}(0)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\beta}\big)(0){\not\!p}_{1}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\Big)~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(44)

The corresponding contribution to
W_{\mu\nu}(q)
is obtained from QCD equation of motion (
344
) and formula (
330
) from
Appendix
9.3
:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm(2b)F}_{1\mu\nu}(q)~=~{4p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over\beta_{q}^{2}s^{2}}\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(45)

Next, as shown in Ref.
[
16
]
, the contribution of the second matrix element in the r.h.s. of the Eq. (
46
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm(2b)F}_{2\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{N_{c}\over 2s}(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta})

(46)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]+\gamma_{\alpha}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\big\}|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

differs from Eq. (
45
) by replacements

p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
,
\alpha_{q}\leftrightarrow\beta_{q}
and exchange of projectile matrix elements and the target ones so we finally get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm(2b)F}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big[{4p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over\beta_{q}^{2}s^{2}}k_{\perp}^{2}+{4p_{2\mu}p_{2\nu}\over\alpha_{q}^{2}s^{2}}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\Big]F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(47)

The third two-gluon contribution to
\check{W}^{F}
in Eq. (
25
) has the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm(2c)F}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\big(g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}-\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}\big)\langle p_{A},p_{B}|\big\{[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]

(48)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+~[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][\Xi_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]+\gamma_{\alpha}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\big\}|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

It is easy to see that after separating color-singlet matrix elements this term is
O\big({1\over N_{c}^{2}}\big)
in comparison to
(
38
) so we neglect it.

Thus, the result for
W_{\mu\nu}^{\rm F}(q)
is the sum of Eqs. (
30
), (
36
), (
41
), and (
47
).
After some algebra, it can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm F}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}W^{\rm fF}_{\mu\nu}(q),~~~~W^{\rm fF}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp}){\cal W}^{F}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp}),

(49)

where

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{\cal W}^{{\rm F}}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp})~=~-g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}+{1\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}(q^{\parallel}_{\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}+q^{\parallel}_{\nu}q^{\perp}_{\mu})+{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}q^{\parallel}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}+{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}[q_{\perp}^{2}-4(k,q-k)_{\perp}]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-~\Big[{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\Big(g^{\perp}_{\nu i}-{q^{\parallel}_{\nu}q_{i}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\Big)(q-2k)_{\perp}^{i}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\Big]

(50)

It is easy to see that
q^{\mu}{\cal W}^{{\rm F}}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp})~=~0
.

4.2
W^{\rm G}
term of Eq. (
27
)

In this section we will repeat the above calculations for the
W^{\rm G}_{\mu\nu}(q)
.
Let us start from

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm G}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{N_{c}g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]|A,B\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(51)

First, as seen from parametrizations (
322
) and (
323
), the leading-twist contribution can be neglected.
Second, as demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
, the contribution of one-gluon gluon operators also vanishes with our accuracy.
Let us now consider two-gluon terms and start with

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm G}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{N_{c}g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}^{n}(x)\Xi_{1}^{m}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]|A,B\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98857pt=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{3}}\Big[\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\Big]~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98857pt=~-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}{{\not\!\!A}}(x){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}{{\not\!\!B}}(0){\not\!p}_{1}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big[1+O\big({q_{\perp}^{2}\over s}\big)\Big]

(52)

where we used formula (
318
) and the fact that

6
6

6

A rigorous argument goes like that: the matrix element (
53
) can be rewritten as

\epsilon_{\nu_{\perp}j}\epsilon_{kl}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)[A_{k}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}l}\psi(x)\rangle~=~\epsilon_{j\nu_{\perp}}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!\!A}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle
.
As demonstrated in Sect.
9.3
,
{\not\!\!A}
in this formula can be replaced by
{\not\!k}_{\perp}
so the contribution is
proportional to matrix element
k^{i}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)i\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle=k^{i}\epsilon_{ij}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\psi(x)\rangle
which vanishes as seen from the parametrization (
328
).

\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\big[A_{k}\sigma_{\star j}-A_{j}(x)\sigma_{\star k}\big]\psi(0)\rangle_{A}~=~0

(53)

It is easy to see that with our accuracy the above equation is the only two-gluon contribution to

\check{W}^{\rm G}_{\mu\nu}(x)
since
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}\Xi_{1}={\bar{\Xi}}_{2}\Xi_{2}=0
and the matrix element

\langle[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]\rangle
is

\sim O\big({1\over N_{c}^{2}}\big)
in comparison to Eq. (
53
) similarly to Eq. (
48
).

Now, using equations (
329
), (
340
), (
342
) and parametrizations (
328
) we obtain the contribution to
photon-mediated hadronic tensor in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm G}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 16\pi^{4}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\star}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}-i\beta_{q}x_{\star}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}\check{W}^{\rm G}_{\mu\nu}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83385pt=~-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{1\over m^{2}}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}H(q,k_{\perp})

(54)

where

H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~=~h_{1}^{\perp f}\big(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}\big){\bar{h}}_{1}^{\perp f}\big(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp}\big)~+~h_{1}^{\perp f}\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}_{1}^{\perp f}

(55)

for the flavor that we are considering.

4.3
W^{\rm T}
contribution of Eq. (
28
)

In this Section we calculate the
W^{\rm T}_{\mu\nu}
term of Eq. (
28
).
The leading-twist contribution

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm T,lt}(x)~=~\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm H,lt}(x)

(56)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\big(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-{1\over 2}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)\langle A,B|[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)]\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\beta}^{~\xi}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

is easily obtained from parametrizations (
328
)
[
21
]
:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}^{\rm H,lt}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},q_{\perp})~=~{1\over 16\pi^{4}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\star}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}-i\beta_{q}x_{\star}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}\check{W}^{\rm lt}_{\mu\nu}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt~=~-\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\big[k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+k^{\perp}_{\nu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\mu}+g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}\big]H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(57)

For the calculation of one- and two-gluon terms it is convenient to consider

\hskip 0.0pt\check{V}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm H}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)|A,B\rangle+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

(58)

and subtract trace to get
\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm T}(x)
afterwards.

4.3.1
One-gluon terms in
\check{V}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm H}(x)

Let us start from one-gluon term coming from
\Xi_{2}
. Sorting out
color-singlet matrix elements, we get

\hskip 0.0pt\check{V}_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(1)H}(x)~=~-{1\over 2s^{2}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(59)

As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
, the only non-negligible contributions are those with one of the indices
in Eq. (
59
) longitudinal and one transverse. For example,
let
\mu
be longitudinal and
\nu
transverse, the opposite case
will differ by replacement
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
.
Using the decomposition of
g^{\mu\nu}
in longitudinal and transverse part (
13
)
we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\Big({2p_{1}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\mu^{\prime}}\over s}+\mu\leftrightarrow\mu^{\prime}\Big)\check{V}_{1\mu\nu_{\perp}}^{\rm(1)H}(x)~=~-\Big({p_{2\mu}p_{1}^{\mu^{\prime}}\over s^{3}}+\mu\leftrightarrow\mu^{\prime}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\big[\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\mu^{\prime}\xi}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~\xi}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~\mu^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\nu\big]~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(60)

As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
, the term proportional to
p_{1\mu}
is small so we get

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\check{V}_{1\mu_{\parallel}\nu_{\perp}}^{\rm(1)H}(x)~=~

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\Big\{i\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}j}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-~\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+~i\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~{2i\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\star{\bullet}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big\}~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(61)

Using Eq. (
292
) one can prove that the contribution of last two terms in the r.h.s. is
\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over s}

and therefore

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{V}_{1\mu_{\parallel}\nu_{\perp}}^{\rm(1)H}(x)~=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\Big[i\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}j}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(62)

\displaystyle\hskip 125.19212pt-~\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big]+\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\star}^{~j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)[B_{\nu}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}-\nu\leftrightarrow j]\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~-~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\langle\bar{\psi}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+x\leftrightarrow 0~=~{ip_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+x\leftrightarrow 0

where we used formulas (
53
) and (
292
).

Using formulas (
329
), (
330
), (
340
), and (
342
)
for quark-antiquark-gluon operators and parametrizations
from Sect.
9.2
we get the contribution to
W_{\mu\nu}
in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptV^{\rm(1)H}_{1\mu\nu}(q)~=~{N_{c}\over 16\pi^{4}}{1\over s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\star}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}-i\beta x_{\star}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}\check{V}^{(1)H}_{1\mu\nu}(x)

(63)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{p_{2\mu}\over\alpha_{q}s}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}~k_{\nu}{(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}H(q,k_{\perp})~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

where terms with replacement
h_{1f}^{\perp}\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}_{1f}^{\perp}
come from
x\leftrightarrow 0
contribution
as usually.

Next, as proved in Ref.
[
16
]
, the term with with
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)
doubles the contribution (
63
) and
the terms with
\Xi_{2}(0)
and
{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(x)
are obtained by the projectile
\leftrightarrow
target replacement, namely
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
,

\alpha_{q}\leftrightarrow\beta_{q}
and
k_{\perp}\leftrightarrow(q-k)_{\perp}
. Thus, the contribution of one-gluon terms to
W^{\rm T}_{\mu\nu}(q)

has the form

\hskip 0.0ptW^{\rm(1)T}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-2\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}~\Big[{p_{1\mu}(q-k)_{\nu}\over\beta_{q}s}{k_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}+{p_{2\mu}k_{\nu}\over\alpha_{q}s}{(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}\Big]H(q,k_{\perp})~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

(64)

4.3.2
Two-gluon terms in
\check{V}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm H}(x)

Let us start with the term

\hskip 0.0pt\check{V}_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2a)H}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)|A,B\rangle+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

(65)

Separating color-singlet contributions, we get

\hskip 0.0pt\check{V}_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2a)H}(x)~=~{1\over 2s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{i}(x)\sigma_{\mu\alpha}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{j}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\alpha}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

(66)

As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
, the contributions from longitudinal
\mu
and transverse
\nu
(or
vice versa
) are small.
For transverse
\mu
and
\nu
we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{V}_{1\mu_{\perp}\nu_{\perp}}^{\rm(2a)H}(x)~=~-{1\over 2s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\mu_{\perp}k}\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi)0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~k}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{1\over s^{4}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\mu_{\perp}}\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

(67)

Using Eq. (
292
) and (
53
), it is possible to demonstrate the second term in the r.h.s. is small and the first term
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{V}_{1\mu_{\perp}\nu_{\perp}}^{\rm(2a)H}(x)~=~-{g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}\over 2s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 25.60747pt+~{1\over s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}\big(A_{k}\sigma_{\star\mu_{\perp}}-{1\over 2}g_{\mu k}\sigma_{\star j}A^{j}\big)(x){1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21495pt\times~\langle{\bar{\psi}}\big(B^{k}\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}-{1\over 2}\delta_{\nu}^{k}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}B^{j}\big)(0){1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big\}+x\leftrightarrow 0

(68)

The corresponding contribution to
\check{V}_{\mu_{\perp}\nu_{\perp}}^{\rm H}(q)
can be obtained
from QCD equations of motion (
340
), (
342
) and parametrization (
347
). We get

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt\check{V}_{1\mu_{\perp}\nu_{\perp}}^{\rm(2a)H}(q)~=~{1\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}N_{c}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big[g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}{k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over 2m^{2}}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(69)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76219pt+~\big[(k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu)(k,q-k)_{\perp}-k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt-~(q-k_{\perp})^{2}k^{\perp}_{\mu}k^{\perp}_{\nu}-{g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}\over 2}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k_{\perp})^{2}\big]{1\over m^{2}}H_{A}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big]

where we introduced the notation

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802ptH_{A}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~\equiv~h_{A}^{f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}){\bar{h}}_{A}^{f}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp})+h_{A}^{f}\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}_{A}^{f}

(70)

Next, consider the case when both
\mu
and
\nu
are longitudinal. The non-vanishing terms are

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{V}_{1\mu_{\parallel}\nu_{\parallel}}^{\rm(2a)H}(x)~=~{4p_{1\mu}p_{2\nu}\over s^{2}}{1\over 2s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{i}(x)\sigma_{\star\alpha}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{j}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~\alpha}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{4p_{1\nu}p_{2\mu}\over s^{2}}{1\over 2s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{i}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\alpha}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{j}(0)\sigma_{\star}^{~\alpha}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(71)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{4p_{2\mu}p_{1\nu}\over s^{2}}{1\over 2s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}k}\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{\star}^{~k}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

Using Eq. (
292
) it is easy to see that the third term in the r.h.s. is
\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over s}
while
the first two terms in the r.h.s. can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}\big(A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star j}-{g_{ij}\over 2}A^{k}(x)\sigma_{\star k}\big){1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+x\leftrightarrow 0

(72)

The corresponding contribution to
V^{\rm H}_{\mu\nu}(q)
yields

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptV_{1\mu_{\parallel}\nu_{\parallel}}^{\rm(2a)H}(q)~=~-{g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over 2Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}{k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int{d^{2}k_{\perp}\over m^{2}}\big[(k,q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-{1\over 2}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\big]H_{A}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(73)

where again we used QCD equations of motion (
340
), (
342
) and parametrization (
347
).

The result for
V_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2a)H}(q)
is the sum of Eqs. (
69
) and (
73
):

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802ptV_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2a)H}(q)~=~{g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}-g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}{k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over 2m^{2}}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(74)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}{1\over m^{2}}\big\{[k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu](k,q-k)_{\perp}-k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~(q-k_{\perp})^{2}k^{\perp}_{\mu}k^{\perp}_{\nu}-{g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}\over 2}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k_{\perp})^{2}-g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\big[(k,q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-{1\over 2}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\big]\big\}H_{A}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

It is possible to demonstrate that the contribution of

\hskip 0.0pt\check{V}_{2\mu\nu}^{\rm(2a)H}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)|A,B\rangle+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

(75)

to
V_{\mu\nu}(q)
doubles the result (
74
) (see Ref.
[
16
]
for proof) so subtracting trace we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm(2a)T}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~2V_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2a)H}(q)-{1\over 2}g_{\mu\nu}2V_{1\xi}^{\rm(2)H,\xi}(q)

(76)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}-g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}{1\over m^{2}}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{2\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}N_{c}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}{1\over m^{2}}\big\{[k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu](k,q-k)_{\perp}-k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~(q-k_{\perp})^{2}k^{\perp}_{\mu}k^{\perp}_{\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k_{\perp})^{2}\big]\big\}H_{A}(q,k_{\perp})

As we will see below, cancellation of terms
\sim g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}
proportional to
H_{A}
in the r.h.s of this equation is actually a consequence of (EM) gauge invariance.

Let us now consider

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt\check{V}_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2b)H}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)|A,B\rangle+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt=~{1\over s^{3}}\Big\{-p_{1\mu}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)A_{j}(x)\sigma_{\nu k}A_{i}(0)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\beta}\big)(0)\gamma^{i}\sigma_{\bullet}^{~k}\gamma^{j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt+~\langle\bar{\psi}(x)A_{j}(x)\sigma_{\mu{\bullet}}A_{i}(0)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\beta}\big)(0)\gamma^{i}\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\gamma^{j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big\}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{4p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over s^{4}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)A_{j}(x)\sigma_{\star k}A_{i}(0)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\beta}\big)(0)\gamma^{i}\sigma_{\bullet}^{~k}\gamma^{j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(77)

where we neglected terms shown in Ref.
[
16
]
to be small. Using Eq. (
297
) the r.h.s. of this equation
can be rewritten as

\hskip 0.0pt\check{V}_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2b)H}(x)~=~-{4p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over s^{4}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!\!A}(x)\sigma_{\star k}{\not\!\!A}(0)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\beta}\big)(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~k}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(78)

so the corresponding contribution to
W_{\mu\nu}
takes the form

\hskip 0.0ptV_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2b)H}(q)~=~-{4p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over\beta_{q}^{2}s^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{1\over m^{2}}k_{\perp}^{2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(79)

where we used Eqs. (
330
) and (
345
).

Next, similarly to Eq. (
64
), the contribution

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt\check{V}_{2\mu\nu}^{\rm(2b)H}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)|A,B\rangle+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

(80)

is obtained by the projectile
\leftrightarrow
target replacement, namely
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
,

\alpha_{q}\leftrightarrow\beta_{q}
and
k_{\perp}\leftrightarrow(q-k)_{\perp}
, and we get contribution to

W^{\rm T}_{\mu\nu}
in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692ptW_{\mu\nu}^{\rm(2b)T}(q)~=~V_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2b)H}(q)+V_{2\mu\nu}^{\rm(2b)H}(q)

(81)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=~-\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big[{4p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over\beta_{q}^{2}s^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}+{4p_{2\mu}p_{2\nu}\over\alpha_{q}^{2}s^{2}}{(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}\Big](k,q-k)_{\perp}H(q,k_{\perp})

Finally, it is easy to see that the two-gluon terms

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm(2c)T}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\big(g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}-\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}\big)\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\psi_{A}^{n}(0)][\psi_{B}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\beta}^{~\xi}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt+~[\psi_{A}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\Xi_{1}^{n}(0)][\Xi_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\beta}^{~\xi}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

(82)

are
O\big({1\over N_{c}^{2}}\big)
so we neglect them.

Summarizing, we get

W_{\mu\nu}^{\rm T}(q)~=~{\rm Eq.(\ref{w1t})+Eq.(\ref{v2otvet})+Eq.(\ref{v34otvet})}

(83)

and adding
W_{\mu\nu}^{\rm G}(q)
from Eq. (
54
) we finally get

W^{\rm H}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~W^{\rm H}_{\mu\nu}(q)+W^{\rm H2}_{\mu\nu}(q)

(84)

The first, gauge-invariant, part is given by

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm H}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}W^{{\rm H}f}_{\mu\nu}(q),~~~~W^{{\rm H}f}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp}){\cal W}^{H}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp})

(85)

where
H^{f}
is given by Eq. (
55
) and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptm^{2}{\cal W}_{\mu\nu}^{H}(q,k_{\perp})~

(86)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}-k^{\perp}_{\nu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\mu}-g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}+2{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}-q^{\parallel}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\Big({q^{\parallel}_{\mu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big[k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+k^{\perp}_{\nu}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\big]+~{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big[k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}-k^{\perp}_{\nu}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}+q^{\parallel}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}\big[q_{\perp}^{2}-2(k,q-k)_{\perp}\big](k,q-k)_{\perp}-~{q^{\parallel}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}+{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}(2k-q,q)_{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}

where
q^{\parallel}_{\mu}\equiv\alpha_{q}p_{1}+\beta_{q}p_{2}
and
{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}\equiv\alpha_{q}p_{1}-\beta_{q}p_{2}
.
It is easy to see that
q^{\mu}W^{\rm H}_{\mu\nu}~=~0
.

The second part is

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm 2H}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}W^{{\rm H}2f}_{\mu\nu}(q),~~~~~~

(87)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{{\rm H}2f}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{1\over Q^{2}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}\bigg[{1\over m^{2}}\big\{[k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu](k,q-k)_{\perp}-k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~(q-k_{\perp})^{2}k^{\perp}_{\mu}k^{\perp}_{\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k_{\perp})^{2}\big]\big\}H_{A}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})+O\big({1\over N_{c}}\big)\bigg]~+~O\big({Q_{\perp}^{4}\over Q^{4}}\big)

where
H_{A}
is given by Eq. (
70
).
These terms are not gauge invariant:
q^{\mu}W^{2}_{\mu\nu}(q)\neq 0
. The reason is that gauge invariance is restored after adding terms like
{m_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}\times{\rm Eq.(\ref{pc})}
which we do not calculate in this paper. Indeed, for example,

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptq^{\mu}W^{2}_{\mu\nu}(q)~\sim~{q^{\perp}_{\nu}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}~~~~{\rm and}~~~q^{\mu}\times~{p_{2}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}^{2}\beta_{q}s^{2}}~=~{q^{\perp}_{\nu}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}

(88)

They are of the same order so one should expect that gauge invariance is restored after calculation of the terms
\sim{p_{2}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}^{2}\beta_{q}s^{2}}
which are beyond the scope of this paper.
For the same reason we see that all structures in Eq. (
19
) except

{g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}

and
{q^{\perp}_{\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}
are determined by leading-twist TMDs
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
.

4.4
Exchange-type power corrections from
J_{A}^{\mu}(x)J_{B}^{\nu}(0)
terms

Power corrections of the “exchange” type come from the terms

\hskip 0.0pt\check{W}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{f}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi^{f}_{1}(x)][{\bar{\Psi}}^{f^{\prime}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Psi^{f^{\prime}}_{2}(0)]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(89)

where
\Psi_{1}
and
\Psi_{2}
are given by Eq. (
16
).
As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]
, the nonzero contributions are

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}_{\mu\nu}~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{A}(x)\big]^{f}\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{2}(0)\big]^{f^{\prime}}

(90)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98857pt+~[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{1}(x)\big]^{f}\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{B}(0)\big]^{f^{\prime}}+~[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{A}(x)\big]^{f}\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{B}(0)\big]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 76.82243pt+~[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{1}(x)\big]^{f}\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{2}(0)\big]^{f^{\prime}}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

with transverse
\mu
and
\nu
.

It is convenient to calculate traceless part and trace separately. Let us start from traceless part.
Separating color-singlet contributions with the help of the formula

\langle{\bar{\psi}}_{m}A^{a}_{i}\psi_{n}\rangle~=~{2t^{a}_{nm}\over N_{c}^{2}-1}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{i}\psi\rangle

(91)

and using Eq. (
315
), we get the corresponding term in
\check{W}_{\mu\nu}
in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}_{\mu\nu}-{g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}\over 2}(\check{W}^{{\rm ex},ff^{\prime}})^{m}_{m}

(92)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{N_{c}\over(N_{c}^{2}-1)s^{3}}\Big(\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\not{p}_{2}\breve{A}_{\mu}(0)\psi(x)+\bar{\psi}(x)\breve{A}_{\mu}(0)\not{p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(x)\rangle_{A}^{f}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\beta}\big)(0)\not{p}_{1}\breve{B}_{\nu}(x)\psi(0)+\bar{\psi}(0)\breve{B}_{\nu}(x)\not{p}_{1}{1\over\beta}\psi(0)\rangle_{B}^{f^{\prime}}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu-{\rm trace}\Big)~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

where we used notations

\breve{A}_{i}\equiv A_{i}-i{\tilde{A}}_{i}\gamma_{5}.~~~\breve{B}_{i}\equiv B_{i}-i{\tilde{B}}_{i}\gamma_{5}

(93)

Using parametrization of matrix elements (
9.4
) and (
9.4
)
we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}(q)-{\rm trace}~=~{s/2\over(2\pi)^{4}}\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}\big(\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}(x)-{\rm trace}\big),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{N_{c}\over(N_{c}^{2}-1)Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}[k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}(k,q-k)_{\perp}]J^{1ff^{\prime}}_{--}(q,k_{\perp})

(94)

where
J^{iff^{\prime}}_{--}(q,k_{\perp})
are defined in Eq. (
450
).

The trace part can be obtained in a similar way. Using Eq. (
314
) one gets

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptg^{mn}\check{W}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}_{mn}~=~{2N_{c}\over(N_{c}^{2}-1)s^{3}}\Big(\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\breve{A}_{m}(0)\not{p}_{2}\psi(x)+\bar{\psi}(x)\not{p}_{2}\breve{A}_{m}(0){1\over\alpha}\psi(x)\rangle_{A}^{f}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\beta}\big)(0)\breve{B}^{m}(x)\not{p}_{1}\psi(0)\rangle_{B}^{f^{\prime}}+\bar{\psi}(0)\not{p}_{1}\breve{B}^{m}(x){1\over\beta}\psi(0)\rangle_{B}^{f^{\prime}}\Big)~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(95)

The corresponding contribution to trace part of

W(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},x_{\perp})
takes the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 2}g^{mn}W_{mn}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}(q)~=~{s/4\over(2\pi)^{4}}\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}g^{mn}\check{W}_{mn}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}(x_{\perp})

(96)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{N_{c}\over(N_{c}^{2}-1)Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}J^{2ff^{\prime}}_{--}(q,k_{\perp})

which agrees with Eq. (6.2) from Ref.
[
14
]
after
replacements
j_{2}=j_{2}^{\rm tw3}-i{\tilde{j}}_{2}^{\rm tw3}
and
{\bar{j}}_{2}=j_{1}^{\rm tw3}+i{\tilde{j}}_{1}^{\rm tw3}
.
It should be noted that the difference between
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
in traceless
vs
trace part is due to difference in formulas
(
315
) and (
314
).

The total contribution of “exchange” power corrections is the sum of Eqs. (
94
) and (
96
), see Eq. (
99
) below.

4.5
Resulting hadronic tensor for photon-mediated DY process

It is convenient to represent hadronic tensor as a sum of three parts

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\gamma}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~W^{1}_{\mu\nu}(q)+W^{2{\rm H}}_{\mu\nu}(q)+W^{3}_{\mu\nu}(q)

(97)

The first, gauge-invariant, part has the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{1}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\big[W^{{\rm F}f}_{\mu\nu}(q)+W^{{\rm H}f}_{\mu\nu}(q)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{{\rm F}f}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp}){\cal W}^{F}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{{\rm H}f}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp}){\cal W}^{H}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp})

(98)

where functions
F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})
and
H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})
are given by Eqs. (
31
) and (
55
)
while

{\cal W}^{{\rm F}}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp})
and
{\cal W}^{{\rm H}}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp})
are presented in
Eqs. (
50
) and (
86
), respectively.

7
7

7

It should be mentioned that
W^{F}
part coincides with the result obtained in Refs.
[
22
,
23
]
using
parton Reggeization approach to DY process
[
24
]
.

Note that

q^{\mu}W^{\rm F}_{\mu\nu}
and
q^{\mu}W^{\rm H}_{\mu\nu}
are exactly zero without any
{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
corrections.
This is similar to usual “forward” DIS, but different from off-forward DVCS where the cancellations of right-hand sides of Ward identities
involve infinite towers of twists
[
25
,
26
,
27
]

The second part is given by Eq. (
87
). It has the same order in
N_{c}
as the first part
but unfortunately is determined by quark-quark-gluon TMDs which are virtually unknown.

The third, “exchange”, part is given by the sum of Eqs. (
94
) and (
96
).

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm ex}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}e_{f}e_{f^{\prime}}W_{\mu\nu}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}(q)~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}^{{\rm ex}ff^{\prime}}(q)~=~{N_{c}\over(N_{c}^{2}-1)Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\big\{[k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}(k,q-k)_{\perp}]J^{1ff^{\prime}}_{--}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21495pt-~g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}(k,q-k)_{\perp}J^{2ff^{\prime}}_{--}(q,k_{\perp})\big\}

(99)

Both second and third part come only from transverse indices.

These terms are not gauge invariant:
q^{\mu}W^{2}_{\mu\nu}(q)\neq 0
. The reason is that gauge invariance is restored after adding terms like
{m_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}\times{\rm Eq.(\ref{pc})}
which we do not calculate in this paper. Indeed, for example,

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptq^{\mu}W^{2,3}_{\mu\nu}(q)~\sim~{q^{\perp}_{\nu}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}~~~~{\rm and}~~~q^{\mu}\times~{p_{2}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}^{2}\beta_{q}s^{2}}~=~{q^{\perp}_{\nu}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}

(100)

They are of the same order so one should expect that gauge invariance is restored after calculation of the terms
\sim{p_{2}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}^{2}\beta_{q}s^{2}}
which are beyond the scope of this paper.
For the same reason we see that all structures in Eq. (
19
) except

{g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}

and
{q^{\perp}_{\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}
are determined by leading-twist TMDs
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
.

In the remaining Sections we will use formulas from this Section as guidelines for calculation of other hadronic tensors in Eq. (
9
).

5
Z
-mediated hadronic tensor

In this Section we will consider the hadronic tensor corresponding to the part of DY cross section mediated by
Z
-boson. Let us start from the symmetric tensor
W^{\rm ZS}_{\mu\nu}
defined in Eq. (
10
)

5.1
Symmetric part of
Z
-mediated hadronic tensor

As in the photon case,
after integration over central fields
{\cal J}^{\mu}
in Eq. (
7
) is replaced by

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-{\cal J}^{\mu}~=~{\cal J}^{\mu}_{1}+{\cal J}^{\mu}_{2}+{\cal J}^{\mu}_{12}+{\cal J}^{\mu}_{21},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{\cal J}^{\mu}_{1}~=~\sum_{f}c_{f}{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{f}\gamma_{\mu}(a_{f}-\gamma_{5})\Psi_{1}^{f},~~~~~{\cal J}^{\mu}_{12}~=~\sum_{f}c_{f}{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{f}\gamma_{\mu}(a_{f}-\gamma_{5})\Psi_{2}^{f}

(101)

and similarly for
{\cal J}^{\mu}_{2}
and
{\cal J}^{\mu}_{21}
.

We start from “annihilation-type” contributions and consider

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm ZSa}_{\mu\nu}~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|{\cal J}^{\mu}_{12}(x){\cal J}^{\mu}_{21}(0)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

(102)

Similarly to the photon case, we will perform calculations for one flavor and sum over flavors later.
After Fierz transformations (
284
), (
286
)
