\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~\sum_{f}e_{f}c_{f}\big[ia_{f}\epsilon_{\mu\nu}^{~~\alpha\beta}\check{\mathpzc{W}}_{\alpha\beta}^{5f}(x)+a_{f}\check{W}^{{\rm as}f}_{\mu\nu}(x)-{i\over 2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu}^{~~\alpha\beta}\check{\mathpzc{W}}_{\alpha\beta}^{{\cal F}f}(x)\big]

where we used definitions (
124
). As demonstrated in Sect.
5.2
,

\check{\mathpzc{W}}_{\mu\nu}^{5f}=\check{W}^{{\rm as}f}_{\mu\nu}=0
with our accuracy
and
\mathpzc{W}_{\alpha\beta}^{{\cal F}f}(q)
is given by Eq. (
150
) so we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I1Aan}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}q^{\alpha}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}~({\tilde{q}}+q_{\perp}-2k_{\perp})^{\beta}\sum_{f}e_{f}c_{f}\mathpzc{F}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(195)

The exchange-type power corrections are

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm I1Aex}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}\langle A,B|(e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}a_{f}+e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}a_{f^{\prime}})[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi_{1}(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Psi_{2}(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 54.06023pt-~e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi_{1}(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

(196)

\displaystyle\hskip 54.06023pt-~e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{1}(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)]^{f^{\prime}}|A,B\rangle-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu-x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}\Big[{e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}a_{f}+e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}a_{f^{\prime}}\over 2}(\check{W}^{{\rm A}ff^{\prime}})^{\rm ex}_{\mu\nu}(x)-~{e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}\over 2}(\check{W}^{{\rm A}ff^{\prime}}_{5{\rm a}})^{\rm ex}_{\mu\nu}(x)-{e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}\over 2}(\check{W}^{{\rm A}ff^{\prime}}_{5{\rm b}})^{\rm ex}_{\mu\nu}(x)\Big]

where we used Eq. (
174
). In the momentum space this gives

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I1Aex}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{iN_{c}\over 2(N_{c}^{2}-1)Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{c_{f}e_{f^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{\mu m}k^{m}(q-k)_{\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]J^{2ff^{\prime}}_{+-}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~c_{f^{\prime}}e_{f}[k_{\mu}\epsilon_{\nu n}(q-k)^{n}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]J^{2ff^{\prime}}_{-+}\Big\}

(197)

where we used Eqs. (
178
) and (
182
).

6.2.1
The result for antisymmetric part of interference hadronic tensor
W^{\rm I1}

As usual, the result consists of “annihilation” and “exchange” parts

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I1A}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~W^{\rm I1Aan}_{\mu\nu}(q)+W^{\rm I1Aex}_{\mu\nu}(q),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I1Aan}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}q^{\alpha}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}~({\tilde{q}}+q_{\perp}-2k_{\perp})^{\beta}\sum_{f}e_{f}c_{f}\mathpzc{F}^{f}(q,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I1Aex}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{\rm r.h.s.~of~Eq.~(\ref{wi1aex})}

(198)

where
\mathpzc{F}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})
is given by Eq. (
127
). Obviously,

q^{\mu}W^{\rm I1Aan}_{\mu\nu}(q)=0
.

6.3
Symmetric part of interference hadronic tensor
W^{\rm I2}

From definitions (
9
) and (
10
) we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm I2S}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}\Big((e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}a_{f}-e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}a_{f^{\prime}})\langle A,B|[{\bar{\psi}}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+~e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\psi}}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\psi(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-~e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\psi}}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi(0)]^{f^{\prime}}|A,B\rangle\Big)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

(199)

where
\psi=\Psi_{1}+\Psi_{2}
in our approximation.

6.3.1
Annihilation-type power corrections

Let us start with annihilation-type power corrections. Since in this case
f=f^{\prime}
, the first term in the r.h.s.
of Eq. (
199
) vanishes and the second turns to

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm I2San}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}c_{f}\check{W}^{\rm If}_{\mu\nu}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm If}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(x)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(0)]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-~[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(x)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(0)]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle-x\leftrightarrow 0

(200)

After Fierz transformation (
287
) it turns to

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm I}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{N_{c}\over 4s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]

(201)

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\gamma_{5}\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{N_{c}g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\Psi_{1}^{m}(x)]|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

where we suppressed flavor label.

Let us start from the second term. Obviously, the leading-twist contribution vanishes with our accuracy.
Next, consider one-gluon terms and start from

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{N_{c}g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|\big([{\bar{\psi}}_{A}^{m}(x)\Xi^{n}_{1}(0)]+[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)\big)[{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]

(202)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\big([{\bar{\psi}}_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Xi^{n}_{1}(0)]+[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)\big)[{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{ig_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{2}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{5}B^{i}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\star i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star i}\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)B^{i}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

The projectile matrix element can bring one factor of
s
while the target one cannot so the contribution to

\check{W}^{\rm I}_{\mu\nu}(x)
is
\sim{g_{\mu\nu}\over\alpha_{q}s}
which is
O(\beta_{q})\times{q_{\mu\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}
.
Similarly, the contributions coming from
\Xi_{2}
and
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}
are
\sim{g_{\mu\nu}\over\beta_{q}s}
and can be neglected.

Let us consider now two-gluon term coming from
\Xi_{1}
and
\Xi_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{N_{c}g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}^{m}(x)\Xi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Xi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}^{n}(0)\Xi_{2}^{m}(x)]|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)(A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\star i}-A^{i}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}){1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{3}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x){\not\!\!A}(x){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0){\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

(203)

where again we used Eq. (
296
) without two last terms and Eq. (
53
).
Now, from equations of motion (
338
) and (
339
) we see that the target matrix element vanishes:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\int\!dx~e^{-i\beta_{q}x_{\star}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0){\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt=~{k^{\perp}_{i}\over\beta_{q}}\!\int\!dx~e^{-i\beta x_{\star}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma^{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~\sim~{k^{\perp}_{i}\over\beta_{q}}s\epsilon^{ij}k_{j}~=~0

(204)

It is easy to see that the term coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}
and
{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}
vanishes for the same reason.

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{N_{c}g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]

(205)

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\psi_{B}^{m}(x)]|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{3}}\langle({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x){\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}(0)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\beta}\big)(0){\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!\!B}(x)\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0~=~0

Next, two-gluon contribution from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}
and
\Xi_{1}
vanishes since
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}\Xi_{1}=0
, and similarly

{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}\Xi_{2}=0
. Finally, the contribution coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}
and
\Xi_{2}
is
\sim{1\over N_{c}^{2}}
as demonstrated in Sect.

4.3.2
(see Eq. (
82
)), so the second term in Eq. (
201
) vanishes and we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm I}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{N_{c}\over 4s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]

(206)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\gamma_{5}\Psi^{n}_{1})(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

This contribution is similar to Eq. (
58
) up to extra
\gamma_{5}
and relative signs.
As we discussed above, extra

\gamma_{5}
cannot change the power of our small parameters
{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
and
\alpha_{q},\beta_{q}
so we can consider only terms
which gave leading contributions to
V^{\rm H}_{\mu\nu}
.

The leading-twist contribution

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm I,l.t.}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{1\over 4s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\psi_{A}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}\psi_{B}(x)]

(207)

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\gamma_{5}\psi_{A}(0)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\psi_{B}(x)]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

is easily obtained from parametrizations (
328
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I,lt}_{\mu\nu}(x)(q)~=~{1\over 16\pi^{4}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\star}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}-i\beta_{q}x_{\star}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}\check{W}^{\rm I,lt}_{\mu\nu}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt~=~-{i\over 2m^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\big(\epsilon_{\mu j}[k^{j}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+(q-k)_{j}k^{\perp}_{\nu}]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big)\mathpzc{H}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(208)

where

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathpzc{H}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~=~h^{\perp}_{1}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}){\bar{h}}^{\perp}_{1}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp})~-~h_{1f}^{\perp}\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}_{1f}^{\perp}

(209)

As usually, the term with
h_{1f}^{\perp}\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}_{1f}^{\perp}
comes from
x\leftrightarrow 0
contribution.

Next, we need to consider terms in Eq. (
206
) with one or two gluon operators and generalize the calculations from Sect.
4.3

to our case.

6.3.2
One-gluon terms

Let us first consider term coming from
\Xi_{1}(0)={i\over s}\sigma_{\star i}B^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi_{A}
. Separating color-singlet
matrix elements in Eq. (
201
), we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(1)}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{i\over 4s^{2}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

(210)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

As we mentioned, this contribution is similar to the one considered in Sec.
4.3.1
so we
need to take only the case
of longitudinal
\mu
and transverse
\nu
, or
vice versa
- all other cases are power-suppressed. We get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(1)}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{ip_{2\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\xi}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}\xi}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{ip_{2\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}j}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~i\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}{\bullet}}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~j}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt+~{2\over s}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}{\bullet}}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\star}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

(211)

where we used power-counting results from
Ref.
[
16
]
to eliminate terms proportional to
p_{1\mu}
, cf Eq. (
61
).
Moreover, similarly to Eq. (
61
) case, Eq. (
292
) shows that two last terms in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
211
)
are small and therefore

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(1)}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{p_{2\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\big[g_{i\nu_{\perp}}\sigma_{\star j}-g_{ij}\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}\big]{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~\big[\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big]\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{p_{2\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)[B_{\nu}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}-B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}]\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}\gamma_{5}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

(212)

where we used
Eqs. (
292
), (
53
), and (
204
).
Next, the target matrix element in the first term in the r.h.s. can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)[B_{\nu}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}-B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}]\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}~=~\epsilon_{\nu j}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0){\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(213)

so we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(1)}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\epsilon_{\nu j}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0){\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

(214)

where we used
\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}\gamma_{5}=i\epsilon_{\nu j}\sigma_{\star}^{~j}
.
Now, from equation of motion (
340
) and parametrization (
328
) we get
the corresponding contribution to

\check{W}^{\rm 1I}_{\mu\nu}(x)
in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(1)}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~-i\epsilon_{\nu j}{p_{2\mu}\over\alpha_{q}sm^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}~k^{j}_{\perp}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\mathpzc{H}(q,k_{\perp})

(215)

The term in Eq. (
206
) coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}
is similar:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(1)}_{2\mu\nu}(x)~=~{i\over 4s^{2}}\big\{\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\sigma_{\star i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}B^{i}(x)\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{ip_{2\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star i}\sigma_{{\bullet}\xi}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt+~\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star i}\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}\xi}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(x)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{ip_{2\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star i}\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(x)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~i\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{p_{2\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\big[g_{i\nu_{\perp}}\sigma_{\star j}-g_{ij}\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}\big]\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(x)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~\big[\langle{\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big]\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{p_{2\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)[B_{\nu}(x)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}-B^{j}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}]\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt+~\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip 45.5244pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\epsilon_{\nu j}\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\sigma_{\star j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0){\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!\!B}(x)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

(216)

where we left only the terms similar to the leading terms in Eq. (
211
)
and made the same transformations.

Now, from equation of motion (
340
) and parametrization (
328
) we get
the corresponding contribution to

\check{W}^{\rm 1I}_{\mu\nu}(x)
in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(1)}_{2\mu\nu}(x)~=~-i\epsilon_{\nu j}{p_{2\mu}\over\alpha_{q}sm^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}~k^{j}_{\perp}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\mathpzc{H}(q,k_{\perp})

(217)

so it doubles the contribution (
215
).

Let us now consider term in Eq. (
206
) coming form
\Xi_{2}
.
For longitudinal
\mu
and transverse
\nu
we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(2)}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{i\over 4s^{2}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(218)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{ip_{1\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star\xi}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~\xi}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}\xi}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\star}^{~\xi}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{ip_{1\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~j}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 125.19212pt-~i\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}\star}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\star}^{~j}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+~{2\over s}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star{\bullet}}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}\star}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

where we neglected contribution
\sim p_{2\mu}
since it is
\sim~p_{2\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}{m_{\perp}^{2}\over\beta_{q}s^{2}}
, see power counting (without
\gamma_{5}
) in Ref.
[
16
]
.
Next, similarly to Eq. (
211
), the last two terms in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
218
) are

\sim O\big({m_{\perp}^{2}\over s}\big)
in comparison to the first two terms so we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(2)}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{p_{1\mu}\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)[g_{i\nu_{\perp}}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}-g_{ij}\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}]\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{1\mu}\over s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\big(A_{\nu}(x)\sigma_{\star j}-A_{j}(x)\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}\big)\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{ip_{1\mu}\over s^{3}}\epsilon_{\nu_{\perp}j}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(219)

From Eq. (
340
) we get the corresponding contribution to
W^{\rm I}_{\mu\nu}(q)
in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 1I(2)}_{1\mu\nu}(q)~=~-i\epsilon_{\nu j}{p_{1\mu}\over\beta_{q}sm^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}~(q-k)^{j}_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}\mathpzc{H}(q,k_{\perp})

(220)

Similarly to Eq. (
215
) one can demonstrate that the term coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}
doubles the result (
220
) of
\Xi_{2}

so we finally get

\hskip 0.0ptW^{\rm 1I}_{\mu\nu}(q)=~-i{2\epsilon_{\nu j}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}m^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}~[\beta p_{2\mu}k^{j}_{\perp}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}+\alpha p_{1\mu}(q-k)^{j}k_{\perp}^{2}]\mathpzc{H}(q,k_{\perp})+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

(221)

where we have added the contribution of transverse
\mu
and longitudinal
\nu
.

6.3.3
Two-gluon terms

Let us start from the contribution to
\check{W}^{\rm I}_{\mu\nu}(x)
of Eq. (
206
) coming from
\Xi_{A}
and
\Xi_{B}
.
After separation of color-singlet matrix elements, it takes the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 2I(1)}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{1\over 4s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(222)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

This equation resembles
\check{V}_{1\mu\nu}^{\rm(2a)H}(x)
of
Eq. (
66
) calculated in Sect.
4.3.2
so we will
use power counting from that Section and
consider only two transverse or two longitudinal indices.

First, let us consider transverse
\mu
and
\nu
.

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 2I(1)}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{1\over 4s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\mu_{\perp}k}\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~k}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(223)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{2\over s}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\mu_{\perp}{\bullet}}\sigma_{\star j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}\star}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

Similarly to Eq. (
67
), from Eqs. (
292
) and (
53
),
we see that the second term in the r.h.s.
can be neglected in comparison to the first one and after some algebra we get

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\check{W}^{\rm 2I(1)}_{\mu_{\perp}\nu_{\perp}}(x)~

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt=~{-1\over 4s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)[g_{\mu j}\sigma_{\star k}-g_{jk}\sigma_{\star\mu_{\perp}}]{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)[g_{\nu i}\sigma_{\bullet}^{~k}-\delta_{i}^{k}\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}]\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt=~{1\over 2s^{3}}\Big[\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)[A_{\nu}(x)\sigma_{\star i}-A_{i}(x)\sigma_{\star\nu}]{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(B_{\mu}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~i}-B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\mu})\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{\nu}(x)\sigma_{\star k}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}(\delta_{i}^{k}\delta_{\mu}^{j}-\delta_{i}^{j}\delta_{\mu}^{k})

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{\mu}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}k}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}(\delta_{i}^{k}\delta_{\nu}^{j}-\delta_{\nu}^{k}\delta_{i}^{j})

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\Big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

(224)

Moreover, it is easy to see that the first term in the r.h.s. can be omitted:
either projectile or target matrix element vanishes due to Eq. (
53
). For the next two terms in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
224
) we use

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt{1\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{\nu}(x)\sigma_{\star k}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}(\delta_{i}^{k}\delta_{\mu}^{j}-\delta_{i}^{j}\delta_{\mu}^{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt=~{1\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)A_{\nu}(x)\sigma_{\star}^{~i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}[B_{\mu}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}-B_{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\mu_{\perp}}]{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt-~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{\nu}(x)\sigma_{\star\mu_{\perp}}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt+~{s\over 4}\langle{\bar{\psi}}A_{\nu}(x)\sigma_{mn}\gamma_{5}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{\mu}(x)\sigma^{mn}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt-~{s\over 2}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)A_{\nu}(x)\sigma_{\mu_{\perp}}^{~j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\big\}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{i}(x)\sigma^{ij}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~=~0

(225)

since the target matrix element in the first term vanishes due to Ee. (
53
), the one in the second term due to Eq. (
204
),
and the last two terms are small by power counting,
\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over s}\big({q^{\perp}_{\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big)
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt{2\over s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{\mu}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}k}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}(\delta_{i}^{k}\delta_{\nu}^{j}-\delta_{\nu}^{k}\delta_{i}^{j})

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt=~\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)[A_{i}(x)\sigma_{\star\nu}-A_{\nu}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{\mu}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{\mu}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt-~{s\over 4}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)A_{\nu}\sigma_{mn}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{\mu}(0)\sigma^{mn}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt+~{s\over 2}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)A^{i}\sigma_{ij}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B_{\mu}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~=~0

(226)

for the same reason: the projectile matrix element in the first term in the r.h.s. vanishes due to Eq. (
53
),
the one in the second term due to Eq. (
204
),
and the last two terms are small by power counting. Thus, we get the result that there is no contribution to

W^{\rm 2I(1)}_{\mu_{\perp}\nu_{\perp}}(q)
with our accuracy.

If both
\mu
and
\nu
are longitudinal,
using formula

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\sigma_{\mu_{\parallel}\alpha}\sigma_{\star j}\otimes\sigma_{\nu_{\parallel}}^{~\alpha}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu~=~-2sg^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\big(\sigma_{\star j}\otimes\sigma_{{\bullet}j}-{1\over s}\sigma_{{\bullet}k}\sigma_{\star j}\otimes\sigma_{\star}^{~k}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\big)

(227)

we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 2I(1)}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{2}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(228)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

where we omitted contribution from the second term in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
227
) due
to power counting coming from Eq, (
292
). Now, using Eq. (
318
) one obtains

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 2I(1)}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{2}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)[A_{i}(x)\sigma_{\star j}-A_{j}(x)\sigma_{\star i}]{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0~=~0

(229)

due to Eqs. (
53
) and (
204
). Thus, we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 2I(1)}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~0

(230)

Similarly, one can demonstrate that the contribution to
\check{W}^{\rm I}_{\mu\nu}(x)
of Eq. (
206
) coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{A}
and
{\bar{\Xi}}_{B}

vanishes.

Let us now consider term coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}
and
\Xi_{2}
. After separating color-singlet contributions, it takes the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 3I}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{1\over 4s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}A^{i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta}\big)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}\gamma_{5}A^{i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta}\big)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

(231)

The power counting for similar Eq. (
77
) in Sect.
4.3.2
shows that we need
to take
\mu
and
\nu
both longitudinal:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm 3I}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~-{p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over s^{5}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\star k}A^{i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta}\big)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\sigma_{\star}^{~k}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\star k}\gamma_{5}A^{i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta}\big)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\sigma_{\star}^{~k}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{ip_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over s^{4}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\star k}A^{i}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta}\big)\gamma_{i}\sigma_{\bullet}^{~k}\gamma_{j}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\big\}~-~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{ip_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}\over s^{4}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x){\not\!\!A}(x)\sigma_{\star k}{\not\!\!A}(0)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta}\big)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~k}\gamma_{5}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~=~0

(232)

where we used formula
\sigma_{\star k}\otimes\sigma_{\bullet}^{~k}\gamma_{5}-\sigma_{\star k}\gamma_{5}\otimes\sigma_{\bullet}^{~k}=0
following from Eq. (
296
). Also, one can demonstrate that the contribution to
\check{W}^{\rm I}_{\mu\nu}
coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{A}
and
\Xi_{A}
vanishes.
Finally, similarly to Eq. (
82
), we can neglect terms
\sim{1\over N_{c}^{2}}

coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1},\Xi_{B}
and
{\bar{\Xi}}_{2},\Xi_{A}
, see the discussion in Sect.

4.3.2
. Thus, we get the result that the contribution to
\check{W}^{\rm I}_{\mu\nu}
coming from two quark-quark-gluon TMDs vanishes with
our accuracy:

\check{W}^{\rm 2I}_{\mu\nu}~=~0

(233)

6.3.4
Exchange-type power corrections

The exchange-type power corrections to eq. (
199
) are

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm I2Sex}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}\langle A,B|\Big((e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}a_{f}-e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}a_{f^{\prime}})[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi_{1}(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Psi_{2}(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91692pt-~e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{1}(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Psi_{2}(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91692pt+~e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi_{1}(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}(0)]^{f^{\prime}}|A,B\rangle\Big)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu-x\leftrightarrow 0

(234)

The terms in the r.h.s. differ from those in
Eqs. (
89
), (
114
), and (
115
) by
replacement of “
+x\leftrightarrow 0
” by
“
-x\leftrightarrow 0
” which leads to change sign of “
\pm
c.c.” terms in
those equations so we get instead of Eq. (
121
)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054ptW^{\rm I2Sex}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{iN_{c}\over 2(N_{c}^{2}-1)Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{(e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}a_{f^{\prime}}-e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}a_{f})[k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+k^{\perp}_{\nu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\mu}

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36967pt+~g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}(k,q-k)_{\perp}]I^{1ff^{\prime}}_{--}(q,k_{\perp})-g^{\perp}_{\mu\nu}(k,q-k)_{\perp}I^{2ff^{\prime}}_{--}(q,k_{\perp})

(235)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt+~c_{f}e_{f^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{\mu m}k^{m}(q-k)_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]J^{1ff^{\prime}}_{+-}(q,k_{\perp})-c_{f^{\prime}}e_{f}[k_{\mu}\epsilon_{\nu n}(q-k)^{n}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]J^{1ff^{\prime}}_{-+}(q,k_{\perp})\Big\}

where
J^{i}_{\pm\pm}
and
I^{i}_{--}
are defined in Eqs. (
450
) and (
451
).

6.3.5
Result for symmetric interference term
W^{\rm I2San}

As usual, we represent the result for hadronic tensor
W^{\rm I2S}_{\mu\nu}(q)
as a sum
of the “annihilation” and “exchange” parts:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I2S}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~W^{\rm I2San}_{\mu\nu}(q)~+~W^{\rm I2Sex}_{\mu\nu}(q)

where the exchange-type corrections are presented in Eq. (
235
) above while

W^{\rm I2San}_{\mu\nu}(q)
is given by
the sum of Eqs. (
208
) and (
221
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I2San}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}c_{f}W^{\rm If}_{\mu\nu}(q),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm If}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-{i\epsilon_{\mu j}\over 2m^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big([k^{j}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{j}k^{\perp}_{\nu}]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{4\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}[\beta p_{2\nu}k^{j}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}+\alpha p_{1\nu}(q-k)^{j}k_{\perp}^{2}]\Big)\mathpzc{H}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

(237)

Let us check gauge invariance of annihilation part of interference hadronic tensor

q^{\mu}W^{\rm I2San}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~0
. First, let us rewrite it in as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm If}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-{i\epsilon_{\mu j}\over 2m^{2}}\Big(\delta_{\nu}^{i}-2{q^{\parallel}_{\nu}q^{i}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\Big)\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}[k^{j}(q-k)_{i}+(q-k)^{j}k_{i}+\delta_{i}^{j}(k,q-k)_{\perp}]\mathpzc{H}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{i\epsilon_{\mu j}\over m^{2}}{{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}[k^{j}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-(q-k)^{j}k_{\perp}^{2}]\mathpzc{H}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

(238)

The integral in the first term is proportional to
(2q_{i}q^{j}+\delta_{i}^{q}q_{\perp}^{2})
and it is easy to see
that

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptq^{\mu}\Big[\epsilon_{\mu j}\Big(\delta_{\nu}^{i}-2{q^{\parallel}_{\nu}q^{i}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\Big)(2q_{i}q^{j}+\delta_{i}^{j})+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\Big]~=~0

(239)

The integral in the second term is proportional to
q^{j}
so

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptq^{\mu}\Big[\epsilon_{\mu j}q^{j}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\Big]~=~0

(240)

and therefore
q^{\mu}W^{\rm If}_{\mu\nu}(q)=0
.

6.4
Antisymmetric interference term of tensor
W^{\rm I2}

Similarly to the symmetric case (
199
),
from definitions (
9
) and (
10
) we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm I2A}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}\Big((e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}a_{f}-e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}a_{f^{\prime}})\langle A,B|[{\bar{\psi}}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+~e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\psi}}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\psi(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-~e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\psi}}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi(0)]^{f^{\prime}}|A,B\rangle\Big)-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

(241)

6.4.1
Annihilation-type power corrections

Again, let us start with annihilation-type power corrections. Since in this case
f=f^{\prime}
, the first term in the r.h.s.
of Eq. (
241
) vanishes and the second can be written as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm I2Aa}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}c_{f}\check{W}^{\rm IAf}_{\mu\nu}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm IAf}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(x)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(0)]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-~[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(x)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(0)]-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

(242)

where we made the usual replacement
\psi\rightarrow\Psi_{1}+\Psi_{2}
.
After Fierz transformation (
289
) the r.h.s. of the above equation turns to

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm IA}_{\mu\nu}(x)~

(243)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~i{N_{c}\over 2s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]-[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\Psi_{1}^{m}(x)]+[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\Psi^{n}_{1}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\Psi_{1}^{m}(x)]|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

where we suppressed flavor label.

Let’s us demonstrate that
\check{W}^{\rm IA}_{\mu\nu}(x)
is small in our approximation.
After Fierz transformation (
289
)
one obtains

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm IA}_{\mu\nu}(x)~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{iN_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|-[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]+[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]-[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

(244)

It is convenient to convolute
W^{\rm IA}_{\mu\nu}(x)
with
\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
and consider

\mathpzc{W}^{\rm IA}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{i\over 2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu}^{~~\alpha\beta}W^{\rm IA}_{\alpha\beta}(x)

(245)

then

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{\mathpzc{W}}^{\rm Ia}_{\mu\nu}(x)~

(246)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{iN_{c}\over 2s}\langle A,B|-[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]+[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]-[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{1}^{n}(0)][{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}^{n}(0)\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}^{m}(x)]|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

This is similar to
\check{W}^{\rm as}_{\mu\nu}(x)
of Eq. (
156
) studied in previous Section. The only difference is
the relative sign between the first and the second term in the r.h.s. of these equations (and replacement of
“
-x\leftrightarrow 0
” by
“
+x\leftrightarrow 0
” which does not change power counting).
Let us qiuckly check that this relative sign does not change the result that the contribution is small.
First, let us consider the one-gluon contribution similar to Eq. (
157
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{i\over s^{2}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(247)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\mu\nu}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)B_{i}(0)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}-x\leftrightarrow 0

As discussed in previous Section after Eq. (
157
), the two terms in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
157
) vanish separately for transverse
\mu
and
\nu
,
are both small for one longitudinal and one transverse index, and the term which changed sign is neglected in Eq. (
159
) so in all cases
the relative sign does not matter. Similarly, one can check this for other one-gluon terms.

Let us now consider two-gluon term coming from
\Xi_{1}
and
\Xi_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{i\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\mu\nu}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt\times~\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}+x\leftrightarrow 0

(248)

Again, this differs from Eq. (
165
) by relative sign between two terms. Looking at the derivation of Eq. (
167
) we see that
both terms in the r.h.s. vanish separately due to Eq. (
53
) so

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{\rm Eq.~(\ref{pizwei3})}~=~{1\over 2s^{3}}\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}[A_{\mu}(x)\sigma_{\star\nu_{\perp}}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}[B_{\mu}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big\}+x\leftrightarrow 0~=~0

(249)

If
\mu
and
\nu
are longitudinal, we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{\rm Eq.~(\ref{pizwei3})}~=~{2i\over s^{5}}(p_{1\mu}p_{2\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu)\big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\star{\bullet}}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\star{\bullet}}\sigma_{\star i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}-x\leftrightarrow 0~=~0

(250)

If now one of indices is longitudinal and the other transverse, the contribution is small due to power counting as discussed
after Eq. (
171
) so the two-gluon term coming from
\Xi_{1}
and
\Xi_{2}
vanishes with our accuracy.
The corresponding contribution coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}
and
{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}

vanishes for the same reason.

Finally, as shown in Eq. (
172
), terms coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}
,
\Xi_{1}
and from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{2B}
,
\Xi_{2B}
are small due to power counting.
Also, as usually we neglect the terms coming from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}
,
\Xi_{2B}
and from
{\bar{\Xi}}_{2B}
,
\Xi_{1}
are
\sim{1\over N_{c}^{2}}
so

\check{\mathpzc{W}}^{\rm IA}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\check{W}^{\rm IA}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~0

(251)

with our accuracy.

6.4.2
Exchange-type power corrections and the result for
W^{\rm I2}

The exchange-type power corrections to eq. (
241
) are

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt(\check{W}^{\rm ex})^{\rm I2A}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over 2s}\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}\langle A,B|\Big((e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}a_{f}-e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}a_{f^{\prime}})[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi_{1}(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Psi_{2}(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91692pt+~e_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi_{1}(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{2}(0)]^{f^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 19.91692pt-~e_{f^{\prime}}c_{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{1}(x)]^{f}[{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Psi_{2}(0)]^{f^{\prime}}|A,B\rangle\Big)-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu+x\leftrightarrow 0

(252)

Similarly to the symmetric case, one can use formulas from Sect.
5.3.3

with change of signs of complex conjugations.
Indeed, the terms in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
252
) differ from those in
Eqs. (
176
) and (
182
) by
replacement of “
+x\leftrightarrow 0
” by
“
-x\leftrightarrow 0
” which leads to change sign of “
\pm
c.c.” terms in
those equations. We get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt(W^{\rm I2A})^{\rm ex}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-{N_{c}\over(N_{c}^{2}-1)Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\sum_{f,f^{\prime}}c_{f}c_{f^{\prime}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{a_{f}[\epsilon_{\mu m}k^{m}(q-k)_{\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]I^{2ff^{\prime}}_{+-}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip 59.75078pt-~a_{f^{\prime}}[k_{\mu}\epsilon_{\nu n}(q-k)^{n}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]I^{2ff^{\prime}}_{-+}(q,k_{\perp})\Big\}

(253)

cf. Eq. (
185
). As usually, exchange power corrections exist only for transverse
\mu
and
\nu
.

Finally, since we proved in previous Section that annihilation-type power corrections
W^{\rm I2A}_{\mu\nu}
vanish, the total result for
W^{\rm I2A}_{\mu\nu}
is equal to the “exchange” part:

W^{\rm I2A}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~(W^{\rm I2A})^{\rm ex}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{\rm r.h.s~of~Eq.~(\ref{wi2aexotvet})}

(254)

7
Results

In this section we will take into account only gauge-invariant terms coming from
annihilation-type terms proportional to TMDs
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
. The reason to neglect
annihilation-type
\sim W^{\rm 2H}(q)
is that the twist-three matrix elements (
347
) are virtually unknown, and exchange-type power corrections can presumably be neglected due to extra
{1\over N_{c}}
.
Anyway, taking into account leading-twist contributions and their “gauge-invariance-restoring”
counterparts appears to a good start for estimations of DY hadronic tensors.

7.1
Hadronic tensors in Collins-Soper frame

In Collins-Soper frame the hadronic tensors are parametrized in terms of
q
and three unit
vectors
X,Y,Z
orthogonal to
q
and to each other. In terms of Sudakov variables they are

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptZ~=~{{\tilde{q}}\over Q_{\parallel}}~=~{1\over Q_{\parallel}}(\alpha_{q}p_{1}-\beta_{q}p_{2}),~~~~~~~X~=~\Big({Q_{\perp}\over Q_{\parallel}Q}q+{Q\over Q_{\perp}Q_{\parallel}}q_{\perp}\Big)

(255)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptY_{\mu}~=~-{1\over Q}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}X^{\nu}Z^{\lambda}q^{\rho}~=~\epsilon_{\mu i}{q_{\perp}^{i}\over Q_{\perp}}

(256)

where
Q_{\perp}\equiv|q_{\perp}|
.

7.1.1
Hadronic tensor for photon-mediated DY process

We parametrize photon-mediated hadronic tensor in a standard way (up to extra
N_{c}
and flavor factors), separately for
W^{{\rm F}f}_{\mu\nu}
and
W^{{\rm H}f}_{\mu\nu}
defined in Eq. (
98
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{{\rm F}f}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-\big(g_{\mu\nu}-{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}\over q^{2}}\big)(W^{{\rm F}f}_{T}+W^{{\rm F}f}_{{\Delta\Delta}})-2X_{\mu}X_{\nu}W^{{\rm F}f}_{\Delta\Delta}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83385pt+~Z_{\mu}Z_{\nu}(W^{{\rm F}f}_{L}-W^{{\rm F}f}_{T}-W^{{\rm F}f}_{\Delta\Delta})-(X_{\mu}Z_{\nu}+X_{\nu}Z_{\mu})W^{{\rm F}f}_{\Delta}

(257)

and similarly

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{{\rm H}f}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-\big(g_{\mu\nu}-{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}\over q^{2}}\big)(W^{{\rm H}f}_{T}+W^{{\rm H}f}_{{\Delta\Delta}})-2X_{\mu}X_{\nu}W^{{\rm H}f}_{\Delta\Delta}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83385pt+~Z_{\mu}Z_{\nu}(W^{{\rm H}f}_{L}-W^{{\rm H}f}_{T}-W^{{\rm H}f}_{\Delta\Delta})-(X_{\mu}Z_{\nu}+X_{\nu}Z_{\mu})W^{{\rm H}f}_{\Delta}

(258)

The expressions for
W_{i}(q)
can be easily obtained from Eqs. (
49
) and (
85
):

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{{\rm F}f}_{T}(q)~=~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\big[1-{q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big]F^{f}(q,k_{\perp}),~~~~~W^{{\rm F}f}_{L}(q)~=~\!\int\!dk_{\perp}{(q-2k)_{\perp}^{2}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(259)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{{\rm F}f}_{\Delta\Delta}(q)~=~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q_{\parallel}^{2}}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp}),~~~~~W^{{\rm F}f}_{\Delta}(q)~=~{Q\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}Q_{\perp}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}(q,q-2k)_{\perp}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{{\rm H}f}_{T}(q)~=~{1\over 2m^{2}Q^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\big(2k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}+[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}](k,q-k)_{\perp}\big)H^{f}(q,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{L}^{{\rm H}f}(q)~=~{1\over m^{2}Q^{2}}\!\int\!dk_{\perp}\big(2k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}](k,q-k)_{\perp}\big)H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\Delta\Delta}^{{\rm H}f}(q)~=~{1\over m^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big({2(q,k)_{\perp}(q,q-k)_{\perp}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}-(k,q-k)_{\perp}+{1\over Q^{2}}\big\{k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+~{1\over 2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}]+q_{\perp}^{2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}-2(q,k)(q,q-k)_{\perp}\big\}\Big)H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\Delta}^{{\rm H}f}(q)~=~-{Q\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}Q_{\perp}m^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}(q,q-2k)(k,q-k)_{\perp}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(260)

These expressions were obtained in Ref.
[
16
]
.

7.1.2
Hadronic tensor for
Z
-mediated DY process

The symmetric part of
W^{\rm ZS}_{\mu\nu}
is given by Eq. (
119
)

\hskip 0.0ptW^{\rm ZS}_{\mu\nu}~=~e^{2}\sum_{f}c_{f}^{2}\big[(a_{f}^{2}+1)W_{\mu\nu}^{f\rm F}(q)+(a_{f}^{2}-1)W_{\mu\nu}^{f\rm H}(q)\big]

(261)

where
W_{\mu\nu}^{{\rm F}f}(q)
and
W_{\mu\nu}^{\rm Hf}(q)
are
expressed in terms of
X
and
Z
vectors in Eqs. (
454
) and (
453
) above.

The antisymmetric part (
183
) can be parametrized as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm ZA}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-2i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}q^{\lambda}\sum_{f}a_{f}c_{f}^{2}\bigg[{Z^{\rho}\over Q_{\parallel}}\mathpzc{W}_{4}^{{\rm F}f}(q)+X^{\rho}{Q_{\perp}\over Q_{\parallel}Q}\mathpzc{W}_{3}^{{\rm F}f}(q)\bigg]

(262)

where

\hskip 0.0pt\mathpzc{W}_{4}^{{\rm F}f}(q)~=\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\mathpzc{F}^{f}(q,k_{\perp}),~~~~~\mathpzc{W}_{3}^{{\rm F}f}(q)~=\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big(1-{2(q,k)_{\perp}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big)\mathpzc{F}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(263)

with
\mathpzc{F}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})
given by Eq. (
127
).

7.1.3
Interference tensors

The symmetric part of the interference tensor
W^{\rm I1}_{\mu\nu}(q)
is given by Eq. (
192
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I1S}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}c_{f}a_{f}\big[W^{\rm Ff}_{\mu\nu}(q)+W^{\rm Hf}_{\mu\nu}(q)\big]

(264)

where
W_{\mu\nu}^{{\rm F}f}(q)
and
W_{\mu\nu}^{\rm Hf}(q)
are
given by Eqs. (
454
) and (
453
) above.

The antisymmetric part of
W^{\rm I1}_{\mu\nu}(q)
is given by Eq. (
198
) which can be represented
similarly to Eq. (
268
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm I1A}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}q^{\lambda}\sum_{f}e_{f}c_{f}\bigg[{Z^{\rho}\over Q_{\parallel}}\mathpzc{W}_{A}^{{\rm F}f}(q)+X^{\rho}{Q_{\perp}\over Q_{\parallel}Q}\mathpzc{W}_{B}^{{\rm F}f}(q)\bigg]

(265)

where
\mathpzc{W}_{A,B}^{{\rm F}f}(q)
are given by Eq. (
263
).

Next, the symmetric part of
W^{\rm I2}_{\mu\nu}(q)
, given by Eq. (
238
), can be parametrized as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm If}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~i\sum_{f}e_{f}c_{f}\Big({Q\over Q_{\parallel}}Y_{\mu}X_{\nu}W_{1}^{\rm If}(q)+Y_{\mu}Z_{\nu}{Q_{\perp}\over Q_{\parallel}}W_{2}^{\rm If}(q)\Big)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

(266)

where

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{1}^{\rm If}(q)~=~{1\over m^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big[{2(q,k)_{\perp}(q,q-k)_{\perp}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}-(k,q-k)_{\perp}\Big]\mathpzc{H}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{2}^{\rm If}(q)~=~{1\over m^{2}q_{\perp}^{2}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\big[(q,k)_{\perp}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-(q,q-k)_{\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}\big]\mathpzc{H}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(267)

Finally, the antisymmetric part of
W^{\rm I2}_{\mu\nu}(q)
vanishes, see Eq. (
251
).

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\rm ZA}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~-2i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}q^{\lambda}\sum_{f}a_{f}c_{f}^{2}\bigg[{Z^{\rho}\over Q_{\parallel}}\mathpzc{W}_{1}^{{\rm F}f}(q)+X^{\rho}{Q_{\perp}\over Q_{\parallel}Q}\mathpzc{W}_{2}^{{\rm F}f}(q)\bigg]

(268)

7.1.4
Angular coefficients

Rewriting the differential cross section (
8
) in terms of hadronic tensors listed in previous Section,
we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptd\sigma~=~{d^{4}q\over q^{4}}{e^{4}\over N_{c}s\pi^{2}}\!\int\!d^{3}ld^{3}l^{\prime}\delta(q-l-l^{\prime})~\mathbb{W}(q,l,l^{\prime})~=~{e^{4}\over 16\pi^{2}sN_{c}}{dQ^{2}\over Q^{2}}dYd^{2}q_{\perp}d\Omega_{l}~\mathbb{W}(q,l,l^{\prime})

(269)

where

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathbb{W}(q,l,l^{\prime})~=~{2\over Q^{2}}L^{\mu\nu}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}[W^{{\rm F}f}_{\mu\nu}(q)+W^{{\rm H}f}_{\mu\nu}(q)]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{2c_{e}^{2}\over Q^{2}}\phi_{1}(Q^{2})\Big\{(a_{e}^{2}+1)L^{\mu\nu}\sum_{f}c_{f}^{2}\big[(a_{f}^{2}+1)W_{\mu\nu}^{f\rm F}(q)+(a_{f}^{2}-1)W_{\mu\nu}^{f\rm H}(q)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~4a_{e}a_{f}c_{f}^{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}l_{\alpha}{l^{\prime}}_{\beta}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}q^{\lambda}\Big[{Z^{\rho}\over Q_{\parallel}}\mathpzc{W}_{1}^{{\rm F}f}(q)+X^{\rho}{Q_{\perp}\over Q_{\parallel}Q}\mathpzc{W}_{2}^{{\rm F}f}(q)\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{4c_{e}\over Q^{2}}\phi_{2}(Q^{2})\sum_{f}c_{f}e_{f}\Big\{a_{e}\big[W^{\rm Ff}_{\mu\nu}(q)+W^{\rm Hf}_{\mu\nu}(q)\big]L^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\epsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}l_{\alpha}{l^{\prime}}_{\beta}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}q^{\lambda}\Big[{Z^{\rho}\over Q_{\parallel}}\mathpzc{W}_{1}^{{\rm F}f}(q)+X^{\rho}{Q_{\perp}\over Q_{\parallel}Q}\mathpzc{W}_{2}^{{\rm F}f}(q)\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{4c_{e}a_{e}\over Q^{2}}\phi_{3}(Q^{2})\sum_{f}c_{f}e_{f}\Big[{Q\over Q_{\parallel}}Y_{\mu}X_{\nu}W_{1}^{\rm If}(q)+Y_{\mu}Z_{\nu}{Q_{\perp}\over Q_{\parallel}}W_{2}^{\rm If}(q)\Big]L^{\mu\nu}

(270)

and

\phi_{1}(Q^{2})\equiv{Q^{4}\over|m_{Z}^{2}-Q^{2}|^{2}+\Gamma_{Z}^{2}m_{Z}^{2}},~~~~\phi_{2}(Q^{2})={(Q^{2}-m_{z}^{2})\over Q^{2}}f_{1}(Q^{2}),~~~~\phi_{3}(Q^{2})={\Gamma_{z}m_{z}\over Q^{2}}f_{1}(Q^{2})

(271)

The angular dependence in the CS frame can be displayed using
the convolutions of leptonic and hadronic tensors are presented in Sect.
9.5
.
One obtains

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\mathbb{W}(q,l,l^{\prime})~=~\sum_{f}\Big\{\big[e_{f}^{2}+c_{e}^{2}(a_{e}^{2}+1)c_{f}^{2}(a_{f}^{2}+1)\phi_{1}+2c_{e}a_{e}c_{f}a_{f}\phi_{2}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\times~\big[\big(W_{T}^{\rm Ff}+{W_{L}^{\rm Ff}\over 2}\big)(1+\cos^{2}\theta)+{W_{L}^{\rm Ff}\over 2}(1-3\cos^{2}\theta)+W_{\Delta}^{\rm Ff}\sin 2\theta\cos\phi+W_{\Delta\Delta}^{\rm Ff}\sin^{2}\theta\cos 2\phi\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt+~\big[e_{f}^{2}+c_{e}^{2}(a_{e}^{2}+1)c_{f}^{2}(a_{f}^{2}-1)\phi_{1}+2c_{e}a_{e}c_{f}a_{f}\phi_{2}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\times~\big[\big(W_{T}^{\rm Hf}+{W_{L}^{\rm Hf}\over 2}\big)(1+\cos^{2}\theta)+{W_{L}^{\rm Hf}\over 2}(1-3\cos^{2}\theta)+W_{\Delta}^{\rm Hf}\sin 2\theta\sin\phi+W_{\Delta\Delta}^{\rm Hf}\sin^{2}\theta\cos 2\phi\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt+~4c_{e}c_{f}\big[2a_{e}a_{f}c_{e}c_{f}\phi_{1}+e_{f}\phi_{2}\big]\Big[\mathpzc{W}_{1}^{{\rm F}f}\cos\theta+{Q_{\perp}\over Q}\mathpzc{W}_{2}^{{\rm F}f}\sin\theta\cos\phi\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt+~a_{e}a_{f}c_{e}c_{f}\phi_{3}\Big[W_{1}^{\rm If}\sin^{2}\theta\sin 2\phi+{Q_{\perp}\over Q}W_{2}^{\rm If}\sin 2\theta\sin\phi\Big]\Big\}

(272)

where
\phi_{i}=\phi_{i}(Q^{2})
and
W^{i}=W^{i}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},Q^{2})
.

The above formula (
272
) is the main result of the paper. It should be compared to
standard representation of angular distribution of DY leptons (
3
)

7.2
Comparison with LHC measurements

The LHC measurements are integrated over the region of invariant mass of DY pair between 80 and 100 GeV.
In this kinematic region the most important contribution comes from terms in Eq. (
272
) multiplied
by
\phi_{1}
. Indeed, for an estimate we can consider
W^{i}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},Q^{2})\simeq W^{i}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},m_{z}^{2})
and get

\hskip-2.84526pt\int_{80}^{100}{dQ^{2}\over Q^{2}}\simeq 0.2,~~\int_{80}^{100}{dQ^{2}\over Q^{2}}\phi_{1}(Q^{2})\simeq 95,~~\int_{80}^{100}{dQ^{2}\over Q^{2}}\phi_{2}(Q^{2})\simeq 0,~~\int_{80}^{100}{dQ^{2}\over Q^{2}}\phi_{3}(Q^{2})\simeq 2.6

(273)

Since the accuracy of our small-x approximation is
\alpha_{q}=x_{A},~\beta_{q}=x_{B}\sim 0.1
we can neglect
all contributions coming form terms not multiplied by
\phi_{1}
.
Moreover, both theoretical
[
28
]
and phenomenological
[
29
,
30
]
analysis indicate that
h_{1}^{\perp}
is is of order of few percent of
f_{1}

and hence in numerical estimates we will disregard the contribution of
h_{1}^{\perp}
.
Introducing notations

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathpzc{W}^{{\rm F}f}(q)~=~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp}),~~~~~\mathpzc{W}^{{\rm F}f}_{L}(q)~=~\!\int\!dk_{\perp}{(q-2k)_{\perp}^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(274)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathpzc{W}^{{\rm F}f}_{1}(q)~=~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{(q,q-2k)_{\perp}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathbb{W}(q,l,l^{\prime})~=~c_{e}^{2}c_{f}^{2}\phi_{1}(Q^{2})\sum_{f}\Big\{(a_{e}^{2}+1)(a_{f}^{2}+1)\Big(\big[\mathpzc{W}^{\rm Ff}-{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q^{2}}(\mathpzc{W}^{\rm Ff}-\mathpzc{W}_{L}^{\rm Ff})\big](1+\cos^{2}\theta)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q^{2}}\mathpzc{W}_{L}^{\rm Ff}(1-3\cos^{2}\theta)+{Q_{\perp}\over Q}\mathpzc{W}_{1}^{\rm Ff}\sin 2\theta\cos\phi+{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q^{2}}\mathpzc{W}^{\rm Ff}\sin^{2}\theta\cos 2\phi\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~8a_{e}a_{f}\Big[{Q_{\perp}\over Q}\mathpzc{W}_{3}^{{\rm F}f}\sin\theta\cos\phi+\mathpzc{W}_{4}^{{\rm F}f}\cos\theta\Big]\Big\}

(275)

where
\mathpzc{W}^{{\rm F}f}_{3}
and
\mathpzc{W}^{{\rm F}f}_{4}
are defined in Eq. (
263
).
Since we neglected exchange-type power corrections we should expect the accuracy of order of
{1\over N_{c}}\sim 30
%.
Let us discuss now some qualitative and semi-quantitative predictions of this equation.
First, let us evaluate
\mathpzc{W}_{i}
at
Q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}
following Ref.
[
14
]
.

7.2.1
Logarithmical estimates of
\mathpzc{W}_{i}
at
Q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}

At
q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}
we probe the perturbative tail of TMD
f_{1}
which is
\sim{1\over k_{\perp}^{2}}

So, as long as
Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}
we can approximate

\hskip 0.0ptf_{1}(\alpha_{z},k_{\perp}^{2})~\simeq~{f(\alpha_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}},~~~~~~{\bar{f}}_{1}\simeq{{\bar{f}}(\alpha_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}}

(276)

(up to logarithmic corrections). Similarly, for the target we can use the estimate

f_{1}(\beta_{z},k_{\perp}^{2})~\simeq~{f(\beta_{z})\over k_{\perp}^{2}},~~~~~~{\bar{f}}_{1}\simeq{{\bar{f}}(\beta_{z})\over k_{\perp}^{2}}

(277)

as long as
k_{\perp}^{2}\ll Q^{2}
. Thus, we get an estimate

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptF^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~\simeq~{F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}},~~~~~F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})~\equiv~f^{f}(\alpha_{q}){\bar{f}}^{f}(\beta_{q})+f^{f}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}^{f},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathpzc{F}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~\simeq~{\mathpzc{F}^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}},~~~~~\mathpzc{F}^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})~\equiv~f^{f}(\alpha_{q}){\bar{f}}^{f}(\beta_{q})-f^{f}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}^{f}.

(278)

Due to Eqs. (
276
) and (
277
), the integrals over
k_{\perp}
are logarithmic and should be cut from below
by
m^{2}_{N}
and from above by
Q^{2}
so we get an estimate

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{1\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}~\simeq~{2\pi\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}},~~~~~~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}~\simeq~-\pi\ln{Q^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}

(279)

where we assumed that the first integral is determined by the logarithmical region
q_{\perp}^{2}\gg k_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}_{N}
and the second by
Q^{2}\gg k_{\perp}^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
.
Taking these integrals to Eq. (
274
) one obtains

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathpzc{W}^{{\rm F}f}(q)~\simeq~{2\pi\over Q_{\perp}^{2}}\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q}),~~~~~\mathpzc{W}_{L}^{{\rm F}f}(q)~\simeq~{2\pi\over Q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big[\ln{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}+2\ln{Q^{2}\over Q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big]F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathpzc{W}_{4}^{{\rm F}f}(q)~\simeq~{2\pi\over Q_{\perp}^{2}}\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}\mathpzc{F}^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q}),~~~~~~\mathpzc{W}^{{\rm F}f}_{1}(q)~\simeq~\mathpzc{W}_{3}^{{\rm F}f}(q)~\simeq~0.

(280)

For numerical estimates of
A_{0}
and
A_{2}
we will need the ratio

r(Q_{\perp}^{2})~\equiv~{\mathpzc{W}_{L}^{\rm Ff}(q)\over\mathpzc{W}^{\rm Ff}(q)}~\simeq~1+2{\ln Q^{2}/Q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln Q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}~~~~

(281)

which does not depend on quark flavors. Substituting these formulas to Eq. (
272
), we get
we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathbb{W}(q,l,l^{\prime})~=~c_{e}^{2}(a_{e}^{2}+1)c_{f}^{2}\phi_{1}(Q^{2})\sum_{f}(a_{f}^{2}+1)\mathpzc{W}^{\rm Ff}\Big\{\Big(\Big[1-{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q^{2}}(1-r)\Big](1+\cos^{2}\theta)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q^{2}}r(1-3\cos^{2}\theta)+{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q^{2}}\sin^{2}\theta\cos 2\phi\big]\Big)+~{8a_{e}a_{f}\over(a_{e}^{2}+1)(a_{f}^{2}+1)}{\mathpzc{F}^{f}\over F^{f}}\cos\theta\Big\}

(282)

7.2.2
Estimates of
A_{0}
and
A_{2}

Since our estimate (
281
) does not depend on flavor, from Eq. (
282
) we get

A_{0}~=~{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over m_{z}^{2}}{1+2{\ln m_{z}^{2}/Q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln Q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}\over 1+{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over m_{z}^{2}}{\ln m_{z}^{2}/Q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln Q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}}

(283)

where we replaced
Q^{2}
by
m_{Z}^{2}
since this function varies slowly between 80 and 100 GeV. This formula is compared to LHC measurements
in Fig.
4
.

Figure 4:
Comparison of prediction (
283
) with lines depicting angular coefficient
A_{0}
in bins of
Q_{\perp}
and
Y<1
from
Ref.
[
18
]
. (long bins) and Ref.
[
17
]
(short bins).

The accuracy of our estimate is about 20% which is reasonable since we neglected corrections
\sim{1\over N_{c}}
.

Next, in our logarithmic approximation

A_{2}~=~{Q_{\perp}^{2}/m_{z}^{2}\over 1+{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over m_{z}^{2}}{\ln m_{z}^{2}/Q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln Q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}}

The comparison with LHC data is shown in Fig.
5
.

Figure 5:
Comparison of prediction (
7.2.2
) with lines depicting angular coefficient
A_{2}
in bins of
Q_{\perp}
and
Y<1
from
Ref.
[
18
]
. (long bins) and
[
17
]
(short bins).

The accuracy here is about 30%.

7.2.3
Qualitative checks for other angular coefficients

First, note that between 10 and 30 GeV the coefficient
A_{1}
from Table 15 of Ref.
[
17
]
is an order of magnitude smaller than
A_{0}
from table 14
(or
A_{2}
from Table 16) in accordance with our estimate
A_{1}\simeq 0
. This is another argument in favor of factorization hypothesis
f_{1}(x_{B},k_{\perp})\simeq f_{1}(x_{B})g(k_{\perp})

which is frequently used in current TMD literature.

Second, from Eq. (
282
) we see that our estimate for
A_{4}
involves non-trivial flavor structure, but one thing immediately seen
is that
A_{4}
does not depend on
Q_{\perp}
in our region 10-30 GeV. As one can see from Table 18 of Ref.
[
17
]
, the experimental numbers for
A_{4}

almost do not change in this kinematical region. Moreover, from Eq. (
278
)
\mathpzc{F}^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})=0
at
Y=0
(i.e., when
\alpha_{q}=\beta_{q}
) so we should expect that

A_{4}
in rapidity bin
2>Y>1
is greater than in bin
Y<1
, and Table 18 confirms that. On the contrary, the results for
A_{0}
and
A_{2}
do not change much between those
rapidity beans in accordance with our prediction that
F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})
does not change radically.

Third, our estimate
A_{3}=0
is in accordance to the fact that experimental numbers for
A_{3}
from Table 17 of Ref.
[
17
]
are order
of magnitude smaller than numbers
A_{4}
from Table 18.

Finally, the coefficients
A_{5}
to
A_{7}
do not appear in our estimate (
272
), again in accordance with Tables 19-21 of Ref.
[
17
]

where the numbers for
A_{5},A_{6}
, and
A_{7}
are actually two orders of magnitude smaller than the numbers for
A_{0}
or
A_{2}
in our kinematical region.

Summarizing, it looks like our
f_{1}
-based estimates point in the right direction, but of course more phenomenological work
is required. Note also that in Ref.
[
16
]
it was demonstrated that TMD evolution in the double-log approximation does not affect the predictions for angular coefficients,
but the single-log corrections to TMD evolution may change the results for asymmetries. The study is in progress.

8
Conclusions and outlook

To my knowledge,
this is the first analysis of DY angular coefficients in the framework of TMD factorization, and, as we saw, the first signs are encouraging.
There are two questions about the TMD analysis of DY process: can we predict/explain angular dependence of DY cross
section from TMD factorization at LHC kinematical range and reciprocally, can we learn something about proton structure from this?

The answer to first question is probably yes.
We see that when we have sufficient knowledge about TMDs responsible for a particular angular coefficient, that
coefficient comes out with reasonable accuracy even with back-of-the-envelope estimates.
For example, the very naive estimate of the coefficients
A_{0}
and
A_{2}

agrees with experiment at 30% accuracy. The error may be due to our approximation of TMDs by perturbative tails or
maybe it is due to
{1\over N_{c}}
corrections proportional to higher-twist operators. I hope that careful analysis
involving established models of
f_{1}(x,q_{\perp})
will distinguish between these two possibilities.
As to the rest of angular coefficients, more details about quark TMDs and at least some guesses about quark-quark-gluon
TMDs are necessary to make quantitative predictions. Also, one needs to take into account fiducial power corrections, see
recent paper
[
19
]
for a review.

This bring us to the second question, namely how much we can learn about proton structure from
Z
-boson experiments.
First, as was demonstrated
above, the coefficients
A_{0}
and
A_{2}
are determined by TMD
f_{1}
with
{1\over N_{c}}\sim 30
% accuracy.
We used the naive logarithmic estimates and it is natural to assume that the realistic models for
f_{1}
will move the curves in Figs.

4
and
5
closer to the experimental points. Also, it should be mentioned, tat the “factorization hypothesis”
for the LT TMDs like
f(x_{B},k_{\perp})~\simeq~f(x_{B})g(k_{\perp})
seems to be confirmed by experimental data in a sense that the
angular coefficients which vanish in this approximation are smaller than the non-vanishing ones.
The coefficients
A_{1},A_{3}
, and
A_{4}
are determined by non-factorized part of
f_{1}
or higher-twist exchange contributions.

Next, the coefficients
A_{5}
-
A_{7}
seem to be determined by exchange-type corrections proportional
to higher-twist quark-quark-gluon TMDs. Unfortunately, comprehensive analysis of exchange-type power
corrections requires calculation of higher-twist contributions restoring EM gauge invariance of these corrections,
see the discussion in Sect.
4.5
.

Let us also discuss the perturbative corrections to asymmetries. As demonstrated in Ref.
[
16
]

our estimates of asymmetries are not affected by summation of Sudakov double logs, but single logs
may bring some changes to tree-level results. It should be also emphasized that,
as discussed in Refs.
[
15
,
14
]
, from the rapidity factorization (
14
) we get
TMDs with rapidity-only cutoff
|\alpha|<\sigma_{t}
or
|\beta|<\sigma_{p}
. Such cutoff, relevant for small-
x
physics, is
different from the combination of UV and rapidity cutoffs for TMDs used by moderate-
x
community, see the analysis in two
[
31
,
32
,
33
]

and three
[
34
]
loops.
This difference in cutoffs does not matter for the tree-level formulas of Sect.
7
, but if one goes beyond the tree level,
one has to relate TMDs with rapidity-only cutoffs to the TMD models with conventional cutoffs. This requires calculations at the NLO level which are in progress.

The author is grateful to A. Prokudin and A. Vladimirov for valuable discussions. This work is
supported by Jefferson Science Associates, LLC under the U.S. DOE contract #DE-AC05-06OR23177
and by U.S. DOE grant #DE-FG02-97ER41028.

9
Appendix

9.1
Formulas with Dirac matrices

9.1.1
Fierz transformations

First, let us write down Fierz transformations for symmetric and antisymmetric combinations

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 2}[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\psi)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]

(284)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{1\over 4}\big(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)\big[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\chi)+({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\chi)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over 4}\big(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-{1\over 2}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)({\bar{\psi}}\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\sigma_{\beta}^{~\xi}\chi)-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\chi)+{g_{\mu\nu}\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{5}\chi)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt{1\over 2}[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\psi)-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]~=~{i\over 4}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\chi)+({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\chi)]

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt-~{i\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\chi)+{i\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\chi)+{i\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\chi)-{i\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{5}\chi)

(285)

Using Eq. (
294
) one can obtain the following formula

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 4}[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\psi)+({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\psi)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{1\over 4}\big(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)\big[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\chi)+({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\chi)\big]

(286)

We need also

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 4}\big[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\psi)-({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\psi)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big]~=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{5}\chi)

(287)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\chi)-{1\over 8}\big[({\bar{\psi}}\sigma_{\mu\xi}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}\chi)-({\bar{\psi}}\sigma_{\mu\xi}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\chi)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big]

for symmetric tensors and

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt{1\over 4}\big[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\psi)+({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\psi)-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big]

(288)

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt=~{i\over 4}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\big[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\chi)+({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\chi)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt{1\over 4}\big[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\psi)-({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\psi)-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big]

(289)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt=~-{i\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\chi)+{i\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\chi)+{i\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\chi)-{i\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{5}\chi)

for the antisymmetric ones.

9.1.2
Formulas with
\sigma
-matrices

It is convenient to define

10
10

10

We use conventions from
Bjorken & Drell
where
\epsilon^{0123}=-1
and

\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\lambda}=g^{\mu\nu}\gamma^{\lambda}+g^{\nu\lambda}\gamma^{\mu}-g^{\mu\lambda}\gamma^{\nu}-i\epsilon^{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}\gamma_{\rho}\gamma_{5}
.
Also, with this convention
\tilde{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}\equiv{1\over 2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}\sigma^{\lambda\rho}=i\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}
.

\epsilon_{ij}~\equiv~{2\over s}\epsilon_{\star{\bullet}ij}~=~{2\over s}p_{2}^{\mu}p_{1}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\mu\nu ij}

(290)

such that
\epsilon_{12}~=~1
and
\epsilon_{ij}\epsilon_{kl}~=~g_{ik}g_{jl}-g_{il}g_{jk}
.
The frequently used formula is

\hskip-2.84526pt\sigma_{\mu\nu}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}~=~(g_{\mu\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}-g_{\mu\beta}g_{\nu\alpha})-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\gamma_{5}-i(g_{\mu\alpha}\sigma_{\nu\beta}-g_{\mu\beta}\sigma_{\nu\alpha}-g_{\nu\alpha}\sigma_{\mu\beta}+g_{\nu\beta}\sigma_{\mu\alpha})

(291)

with variations

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2\over s}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\sigma_{\star j}~=~g_{ij}-i\epsilon_{ij}\gamma_{5}-i\sigma_{ij}-{2i\over s}g_{ij}\sigma_{{\bullet}\star},~~~{2\over s}\sigma_{\star i}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}~=~g_{ij}+i\epsilon_{ij}\gamma_{5}-i\sigma_{ij}+{2\over s}g_{ij}\sigma_{\star{\bullet}},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\sigma_{ij}\sigma_{{\bullet}k}=-\sigma_{{\bullet}k}\sigma_{ij}~=-ig_{ik}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}+ig_{jk}\sigma_{{\bullet}i},~~~\sigma_{ij}\sigma_{\star k}=-\sigma_{\star k}\sigma_{ij}~=-ig_{ik}\sigma_{\star j}+ig_{jk}\sigma_{\star i}

(292)

We need also the following formulas with
\sigma
-matrices in different matrix elements

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\tilde{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}\otimes\tilde{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}~=~-{1\over 2}(g_{\mu\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}-g_{\nu\alpha}g_{\mu\beta})\sigma_{\xi\eta}\otimes\sigma^{\xi\eta}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~g_{\mu\alpha}\sigma_{\beta\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}-g_{\nu\alpha}\sigma_{\beta\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\mu}^{~\xi}-g_{\mu\beta}\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}+g_{\nu\beta}\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\mu}^{~\xi}-\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\otimes\sigma_{\mu\nu}

(293)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\tilde{\sigma}_{\mu\xi}\otimes\tilde{\sigma}_{\nu}^{~\xi}~=~-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2}\sigma_{\xi\eta}\otimes\sigma^{\xi\eta}+\sigma_{\nu\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\mu}^{~\xi},~~~~\sigma_{\xi\eta}\otimes\tilde{\sigma}^{\xi\eta}~=~\tilde{\sigma}_{\xi\eta}\otimes\sigma^{\xi\eta}

(294)
